# The Roman Canon in German Evangelical Masses (1522–1544)

This file gives the offertory and eucharistic rites of the orders in the Sehling corpus
that keep pieces of the Roman Canon and of the priest's prayers of the Ordinary, or that
put a prayer of their own in the Canon's place. They were found by a search of the whole
corpus (§1.3) and are printed in order of date.

| § | Order | Sehling | `eko.db` doc | Pages |

| 2 | **Nördlingen**, Kaspar Kantz, *Von der Evangelischen Mesß*, 1522 (and 1524) | 12, VIII.1 | 369 | 285–288 |
| 3 | **Wittenberg**, Luther, *Formula missae*, 1523 | 1, Luther Nr. 4 | 2 | 4–9 |
| 4 | **Allstedt**, Müntzer, *Deutsch evangelisch Messe* and *Ordnung*, 1523/24 | 1, Nr. 46–47 | 51, 52 | 500–501, 504–506 |
| 5 | **Strasbourg**, the early German Masses, 1524–1525 | 20, Straßburg Nr. 3 | 1279 | 120–162 |
| 6 | **Worms**, *Deutsche Messe*, 1524 | 19, III, Nr. 1 | 1066 | 127–129 |

Each order is printed from the point where the offertory stood (after the Creed or the
sermon) to the dismissal. For Kantz, the Kiel Mass and Bugenhagen, the priest's preparation
at the altar is included as well. The original comes first and the English follows. Annotations
name the Roman text each piece comes from.

**Conventions**

- **Original text.** This is Sehling's base text. His footnote numbers, sigla and variant
  apparatus are removed, and so are page and folio markers. Line-break hyphens are joined.
  Spelling, punctuation and Sehling's own supplied letters in square brackets (e.g.
  *Nemen[t]*) are kept. A cross (✠) marks where the print has a sign of the cross over the
  elements. Rubrics are in italics. "[…]" marks an omission (a hymn or lesson printed in
  full, or text given in another section). A few plain misprints are corrected, and the
  notes say where.
- **English.** The translation is formal-equivalence, in the idiom of the Authorized
  Version: word order and clause structure are kept where English allows, and nothing is
  smoothed away.
- **Roman comparison texts.** These are the standard texts of the late-medieval and
  Tridentine *Ordo Missae*. Diocesan missals of the 1520s–40s differ in small details. Where
  a German text follows a wording the Tridentine missal does not have, this is noted, but it
  is not attributed to a particular missal.

## 1. Summary of findings

The orders printed here run from Kantz's Nördlingen Mass of 1522 to Bugenhagen's
Hildesheim order of 1544. Between them they show what the first generation of evangelical Masses kept
of three things:

- the Roman Canon (*Te igitur* to the doxology *Per ipsum*);
- the prayers of the Ordinary around it: the offertory prayers, the embolism, the priest's
  prayers before and after communion, and the words of distribution;
- the rites that go with them: elevation, fraction, the Peace.

§1.1 summarises the findings in the order of the Mass. §1.2 gives the concordance tables.
§1.3 says how the corpus was searched and which orders were examined but not printed.

### 1.1 Findings

**The offertory.**

- **The offertory prayers.** Luther rejected everything that "sounds of oblation"
  (§3.1). No order keeps *Suscipe, sancte Pater*, *Offerimus tibi* or *Suscipe, sancta
  Trinitas*.
- **What does survive:**
  - **the offertory chant**: Latin in Brandenburg 1540 (§12.1); Müntzer's German *Ad te
    levavi* (§4.1); a German hymn "for the offertory" in Lippe (§10) and Calenberg-Göttingen
    1542 (§13.1);
  - **a rubric for preparing the chalice**: Döber (§8.1), Volprecht (§7.2), Bugenhagen's
    orders (§15.1);
  - **the *Orate fratres*, recast** as a request for prayer: Worms ("mein und ewer gebet",
    §6.1), Kantz and Bremen (§2.1, §9.2), and Strasbourg, where it becomes a bidding on
    Rom 12:1 to offer "our bodies" (§5.1, §5.3);
  - ***Veni, sanctificator* replaced** by the Whitsun antiphon *Veni, Sancte Spiritus*
    and a collect: Kantz (§2.2), Bremen, Kiel (§11.1);
  - **the Secret's closing *Per omnia saecula saeculorum. Amen***, sung before the Preface
    dialogue: Müntzer (§4.1), Lippe (§10), Göttingen (§13.1).
- **One prayer over the gifts.** Only Pfalz-Neuburg 1543 has one: Osiander's prayer "Wir
  bringen … dise deine gaben" (§14.2). It borrows from *Quam oblationem*, *Unde et memores*
  and *Supplices*, and asks for the Spirit. Sehling's editor, after P. Graff, calls it the
  first Lutheran order with a kind of epiclesis (Sehling 8, p. 45, n. 12).

**The Preface and Sanctus.**

- **The common Preface in German:** Worms, Kantz, Bremen.
- **Proper Prefaces in German:** Müntzer (Blessed Virgin, reworded so that God, not Mary,
  is praised), Strasbourg (Cross), Lippe (Trinity), Kiel and Göttingen (Easter).
- **Luther's short Preface.** Luther cut the Preface off at *per Christum Dominum nostrum*
  and joined the Verba to it (§3.2). The Nürnberg Latin Mass (§7.1), Döber (§8.2) and
  Volprecht (§7.2) follow him.
- **The Sanctus after the Verba:** Luther, Döber, Volprecht and Pfalz-Neuburg.

**The Canon.**

- **The only continuous Canon is Worms 1524** (§6.3–6.9). It runs from *Te igitur* to
  *Supplices te rogamus* in German, abridged:
  - there are no saints in *Communicantes* (only its closing clause), no *Memento* of the
    dead, no *Nobis quoque* and no *Per ipsum*;
  - the sacrificial nouns become "bread and wine" and "our prayer";
  - emperor and king replace pope and bishop.
- **Elsewhere single Canon prayers survive:**
  - ***Quam oblationem***, recast as a petition that the bread and wine "may become unto
    us" the body and blood: Kantz (§2.4). Bremen changes it to a prayer that "we may firmly
    believe" this (§9.3).
  - ***Unde et memores***, as a thanksgiving "after the elevation": Strasbourg (§5.1). A
    later hand wrote the Worms text of the prayer into the Strasbourg manuscript.
- **The Canon's intercession** (the petitions of *Te igitur* and the *Memento*) survives in
  three ways:
  - **in the Canon's own place after the Sanctus:** Strasbourg (§5.1); an optional prayer
    in Döber taken from Strasbourg (§8.11); four collects in Brandenburg (§12.2); three in
    Pfalz-Neuburg (§14.4); one in Göttingen (§13.1);
  - **as one long prayer "anstatt des Canon":** Strasbourg 1525 (§5.2);
  - **as the general prayer after the sermon,** which Bugenhagen calls "unse Canon" (§15.4).
- **The Canon critique.** The Brandenburg-Nürnberg order of 1533, repeated in Brandenburg
  1540 and Pfalz-Neuburg 1543, demands that the offering clauses of the Canon be replaced
  by "Wir gedenken des opfers deines einigen Sons" (Appendix). Sehling's register points to
  this critique under "Embolismus" (11:183, 13:65), because his note there explains the
  Würzburg embolism's added saints.

**The words of institution.**

- **Scripture or Canon.** Most orders read the Verba from 1 Cor 11 or a harmony of the
  Gospels. A distinct line keeps the Canon's own *Qui pridie*: *elevatis oculis*,
  *benedixit*, *novi et aeterni testamenti*, sometimes *mysterium fidei*. The line runs
  through Kantz, the Nürnberg Latin Mass, Schwarz at Strasbourg, Müntzer, Bremen, Lippe
  and Kiel.
- **Where Müntzer's wording travelled.** It reached Lippe, Kiel and Göttingen's proper
  Masses through the Erfurt *Kirchenämter* of 1525–26 (§10, §13).
- **The elevation** is kept by Luther (at the *Benedictus*), Worms, the Nürnberg Latin
  Mass, Müntzer, Strasbourg (cup only), Brandenburg and Pfalz-Neuburg. Kantz moves it to the
  communion ("Secht …"). Schleswig-Holstein 1542 permits it.

**The Our Father and the embolism.**

- ***Praeceptis salutaribus moniti*** is recast in German almost everywhere, e.g. "Last
  uns beten, wie uns der herr Christus Jesus befohlen hat …".
- **The embolism *Libera nos, quaesumus*.** Luther struck it out. What remains elsewhere:
  - **its conclusion only** ("Durch unsern Herren Jesum … lebt und herrscht"): Worms,
    Kantz, Bremen, Lippe;
  - **its sung ending *Per omnia saecula*** ("Durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit"): Müntzer,
    Lippe;
  - **one line of its body** ("O Herr, erlös uns von allen sichtigen und unsichtigen
    feinden …"): Döber (p. 54; Sehling: "Rest des Embolismus") and Strasbourg.

**The fraction.**

- **Forbidden.** Luther forbids the fraction and commixture: "nec frangatur hostia nec in
  calicem misceatur" (§3.3). Worms repeats the prohibition (§6.11).
- **Kept in two orders only**, at the Roman place (the close of the embolism, before the
  Peace), and neither with the commixture:
  - the 1524 edition of Kantz's Mass, "Hie neme der priester das brot und breche es, so vil
    ers bedarf" (§2.5);
  - Müntzer's Allstedt order, where the priest "der communicanten halben das sacrament
    teilet" (§4.3).
- **The *Deutsche Messe*** (1526) has no fraction.
- **Reformed breaking of bread.** Every other mention of breaking the bread in the corpus
  comes from Reformed orders from the 1550s on (à Lasco, the Palatinate, Anhalt, the Upper
  Palatinate, Hessen-Kassel). There the bread is broken at the distribution on the ground
  of 1 Cor 10:16 and Acts 2:42. It is a new rite, not a survival.

**The Peace and the *Agnus Dei*.**

- ***Pax Domini sit semper vobiscum*** survives widely: Worms, Luther (as a "public
  absolution"), the Nürnberg Latin Mass, Müntzer, Volprecht, Brandenburg, Pfalz-Neuburg,
  Lippe and Göttingen. Kantz moves it into the communion.
- **The *Agnus Dei*** is kept in German or Latin by nearly all. Luther has it sung during
  communion.

**The priest's prayers before communion.**

- **All three kept.** *Domine Iesu Christe, qui dixisti*, *Domine Iesu Christe, Fili Dei
  vivi* and *Perceptio Corporis tui* stay in Latin in Brandenburg (§12.5) and Pfalz-Neuburg
  (§14.6). Volprecht has all three in German (§7.2).
  - The "Libera nos" in these orders is the petition inside *Fili Dei vivi* ("Libera nos
    per hoc sacrosanctum corpus et sanguinem tuum"), made plural. It is not the embolism.
- ***Fili Dei vivi* alone** is kept in German by:
  - Kantz and Bremen, abridged. Bremen changes "by this thy holy body and blood" to "by thy
    bottomless mercy".
  - Strasbourg, prayed by all.
  - Döber and Kiel, said by the people line by line after the priest (§8.8, §11.3).
- ***Domine, non sum dignus*** appears once, in German, in Kiel (§11.3).
- ***Qui dixisti*** becomes the closing collect of the Mass in Göttingen (§13.3).

**The words of distribution.**

- **The *custodiat*** (*Corpus Domini nostri Iesu Christi custodiat animam tuam in vitam
  aeternam*) is the most widespread survival. It appears in about thirty orders of the
  corpus, from Worms and Volprecht (1524) to 1609:
  - Worms, Döber, Kantz, Bremen, the Nürnberg *Form und Ordnung*, Göttingen and many
    later orders;
  - most in the wording of the Albertine Saxon Agenda of 1539, "Der leib unsers herrn Jesu
    Christi, fur dich in tod gegeben, sterke und beware dich im glauben zum ewigen leben";
  - Brandenburg 1540 uses it only for the communion of the sick (Sehling 3, pp. 79–80).
- ***Calicem salutaris accipiam*** (Ps 116:13) survives in Worms, Kantz, Bremen and
  Volprecht.

**After communion.**

- ***Quod ore sumpsimus* and *Corpus tuum, Domine*.** Luther made these two ablution
  prayers the postcommunion (§3.5). Brandenburg and Pfalz-Neuburg keep them in Latin;
  Volprecht has both in German; Strasbourg has *Quod ore* as its *complenda*.
- **Roman postcommunions and collects reused:**
  - Döber's *Largire sensibus nostris* (Wednesday in Holy Week). Sehling thought Döber's
    prayer newly composed; the identification is new here.
  - the Trinity *Proficiat*: Volprecht, Lippe;
  - the collect *Protector in te sperantium*: Kantz.
- **The priest's final *Placeat tibi, sancta Trinitas*** survives in Kantz, the sacrifice
  turned into "unser lob und danksagung" (§2.7).
- **The *Nunc dimittis*** follows communion in Worms, Döber, Kantz and Bremen.

**The priest's preparation and thanksgiving.**

- **Bugenhagen's preparatory prayer** is the evangelical counterpart of the prayers at the
  foot of the altar (§15):
  - in Wolfenbüttel 1543 and Hildesheim 1544, the priest kneels with the sexton and prays
    the *Pater noster* and the versicles *Domine, non secundum peccata nostra … Adiuva nos,
    Deus*;
  - in Schleswig-Holstein 1542 he says the *Confiteor* privately and prays for preachers,
    king and realm.
- **After Mass,** Hildesheim and Schleswig-Holstein have him kneel again and "thank God
  secretly for himself".

**How the orders are related.**

- **Kantz** → Bremen (a Low German version) and the opening prayers of Kiel.
- **Müntzer** → (Erfurt *Kirchenämter*) → Lippe, Kiel, Göttingen.
- **Strasbourg** → Worms (the same Mass) and Döber's optional intercession.
- **Luther's *Formula missae*** → the Nürnberg Latin Mass, Döber, Volprecht, Brandenburg.
- **Nürnberg 1533** → the exhortation and Canon critique of Brandenburg 1540 and
  Pfalz-Neuburg 1543.
- **Brandenburg 1540** → Calenberg-Göttingen 1542.

### 1.2 Concordance tables

One row per order, in date order; the section number is given with each. "—" means absent;
*italic* means a rubric only or a permission. "Canon form" of the Verba means the wording
of *Qui pridie* (*elevatis oculis*, *benedixit*, *novi et aeterni testamenti*), as against
the wording of 1 Cor 11 and the Gospels.

**Table 1. Offertory, Preface, Sanctus**

| Order | Offertory chant | Offertory prayers, *Orate fratres* | The Secret's *Per omnia saecula* | Preface | Sanctus |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kantz 1522 (§2) | — | *Orate fratres* recast; *Veni Sancte Spiritus* for *Veni sanctificator* | — | German, common | German, before the Canon |
| Luther, *FM* 1523 (§3) | — | *forbidden* (all "sounds of oblation") | — | Latin, cut at *per Christum*, runs into the Verba | after the Verba |
| Müntzer 1523/24 (§4) | *Ad te levavi*, German | — ("Wir halten kein opfer") | "durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit. Amen" | German, proper (Blessed Virgin, reworded) | German, before |
| Strasbourg 1524–25 (§5) | — | bidding on Rom 12:1 for *Orate fratres*; *lavabo* kept | — | German, proper (Cross); 1525 common; last form none | German, before; last form none |
| Worms 1524 (§6) | — | *Orate fratres*, German ("mein und ewer gebet") | — | German, common | German, before |
| Nürnberg parishes 1524, *Form* 1525 (§7.1, §7.3) | *omitted* | *omitted* | — | Latin, cut at *per Christum* | Latin, during or after *Qui pridie* |
| Volprecht 1524 (§7.2) | — | *chalice prepared* | — | German, cut, runs into the Verba | after the Verba |
| Döber 1525 (§8) | — | *chalice prepared after the Gradual* | — | German, cut (Luther's form) | German, after the Verba |
| Bremen 1525 (§9) | — | as Kantz | — | as Kantz | before |
| Lippe [1525–38] (§10) | German hymn | — | "Durch alle ewicheit der ewicheit amen" | German, Trinity | before |
| Kiel [after 1526] (§11) | — | Kantz's *Veni* and collect, before the altar | — | German, Easter | (text breaks off) |
| Brandenburg 1540 (§12) | Latin; German psalm in villages | — | — | Latin, of the day | Latin, four German collects under it |
| Calenberg-Göttingen 1542 (§13) | German psalm "anstat des offertorii" | — | "Gott sey preis von ewigkeit zu ewigkeit. Amen" | German, Easter | Luther's German Sanctus, a collect under it |
| Pfalz-Neuburg 1543 (§14) | — | Osiander's prayer over the gifts ("Wir bringen … dise deine gaben") | — | — | after the Verba, three collects under it |
| Bugenhagen 1528–44 (§15) | German hymn while bread and wine are prepared | — | — | Latin, on feasts only | Latin or German, on feasts |

| Müntzer 1523/24 (§4) | — | — | — | new | *Benedicamus* |
| Strasbourg 1524–25 (§5) | *Fili Dei vivi*, German, prayed by all | 1525: "Gedenckent, glaubent, verkündent …" | *Quod ore*, German | *Quod ore* as *complenda* | Trinitarian blessing |
| Worms 1524 (§6) | — | *custodiat*, German; *Calicem salutaris* | echoed in the postcommunion | German, from *Perceptio* and *Corpus tuum* | *Nunc dimittis*; Trinitarian blessing |
| Nürnberg parishes 1524, *Form* 1525 (§7.1, §7.3) | — | 1525: *custodiat*, German | — | proper *complenda* | 1525: *Benedicamus* |
| Volprecht 1524 (§7.2) | *Qui dixisti*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Perceptio*, German | *custodiat* (priest); *Calicem salutaris* | both, German | Trinity *Proficiat* | Aaronic blessing; *Benedicamus* |
| Döber 1525 (§8) | *Fili Dei vivi*, German, said by the people | *custodiat*, German | — | *Largire sensibus nostris* | *Nunc dimittis*; Aaronic and Trinitarian blessing |
| Bremen 1525 (§9) | *Fili Dei vivi*, "dorch dijne grondelose barmherticheit" | *custodiat*; *Calicem salutaris* | — | — | *Nunc dimittis*; *Te Deum* |
| Lippe [1525–38] (§10) | — | — | — | Trinity *Proficiat* | *Benedicamus*; Aaronic blessing |
| Kiel [after 1526] (§11) | *Fili Dei vivi*, said by the people; *Domine, non sum dignus* | — | — | a collect | Aaronic blessing |
| Brandenburg 1540 (§12) | *Qui dixisti*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Perceptio*, Latin | "Nim hin und iss …" (*custodiat* only for the sick) | both, Latin | Nürnberg 1533 thanksgiving | — |
| Calenberg-Göttingen 1542 (§13) | — | *custodiat*, German | — | Brandenburg thanksgiving | *Qui dixisti* as the final collect; Easter *Benedicamus*; Aaronic blessing |
| Pfalz-Neuburg 1543 (§14) | *Qui dixisti*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Perceptio*, Latin | "Nimm hin und iß …" | both, Latin | the same thanksgiving, split in two | *Benedicamus*; Aaronic or other blessings |
| Bugenhagen 1528–44 (§15) | — | none: nothing is said at the distribution | — | Luther's "Wy danken dy" | Aaronic blessing; private thanksgiving (1542, 1544) |

### 1.3 Method, and orders examined but not printed

The corpus (`eko.db`, all volumes of Sehling) was searched with regular expressions for:

- the priest's prayers *Qui dixisti*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Perceptio*, *Domine non sum
  dignus*, *Corpus … custodiat*, *Quod ore sumpsimus* and *Corpus tuum*;
- Canon incipits and the Canon form of the Verba ("hub auf seine augen", "neuen und
  ewigen");
- offertory language and blessing of the gifts;
- the fraction ("breche", "teilet", *frangatur*, *fractio*);
- the embolism;
- Sehling's register entries *Offertorium*, *Kanongebet*, *Messkanon*, *canon missae*,
  *Epiklese*, *Konsekration* and *Postcommunio*.

Every hit was read in context. The following orders were examined and are not printed:

| Order | Why not printed |
|---|---|
| Luther, *Deutsche Messe*, 1526 (Sehling 1, doc 3) | No Canon or Ordinary prayers (see §3.6). |
| Homberg, *Reformatio ecclesiarum Hassiae*, 1526 (Sehling 8, doc 2247, pp. 44–45) | Rules only: "Canon ille missarius, et universae orationes, in quibus reperitur sacrificii aut hostiae vox a nemine ultra in hac coena dicatur". Luther's German Mass is prescribed. |
| Braunschweig 1528, Hamburg 1529 / Lübeck 1531, Pomerania 1535 (Bugenhagen) | Latin Preface and Sanctus on feasts only, otherwise as §15. |
| Heilbronn 1530 (Sehling 17, doc 770, pp. 276–277) | Quotes *Te igitur*, *Quam oblationem* and *Supra quae* only to refute them. |
| Transylvania, Honterus, 1543/47 (Sehling 24, docs 1669–1670) | "reiecto canone": Preface, Sanctus, Our Father and Verba, with the Saxon *custodiat* (1547). |
| Corvinus, *Kirchenordnung* 1542 (Sehling 21, doc 1460) | Luther's paraphrase and the Verba. The distribution "Nym hen, edt und gedencke …" is Strasbourg's "Gedenckent, glaubent" type (§5.2), not the *custodiat*. |
| Schwäbisch Hall 1543 (Sehling 17, doc 762) and its communion of the sick, 1537 (doc 761) | *Custodiat* only ("Der Leib unsers Herrn Christi bewar dich zum ewigen leben"). |
| Later Upper German orders, e.g. Sehling 16, V, 1560 (doc 742), after Basel 1526 | A prayer before communion that echoes the *Perceptio*: "uf das wir dein Nachtmal uns nit zum gericht empfahen, sonder zu dem ewigen leben". |
| Reformed orders (Kurpfalz from 1563, Anhalt, Hessen-Kassel, à Lasco) | No Canon prayers; the breaking of bread is a new rite (§1.1). |

---

## 2. Nördlingen, Kaspar Kantz, *Von der Evangelischen Mesß*, 1522

*Von der Evangelischen Mesß. Mit schönen Christlichen Gebetten vor und nach der empfahung
des Sacraments. Durch Caspar Kantz von Nördlingen.* Sehling 12, VIII.1, pp. 285–288
(`eko.db` doc 369). Sehling's base text is the print of 1522. The apparatus gives the
changes of the second edition, *Die rechte evangelische und apostolische Mesß*, 1524
("II").

This is the earliest German evangelical Mass in the corpus. It is the source of the
Bremen Mass of 1525 (§9), and its opening prayers reappear in the Kiel Low German Mass
(§11). The Mass has no offertory. In its place Kantz puts:

- an address and absolution;
- a recast *Orate fratres*;
- the *Veni Sancte Spiritus* antiphon.

Then comes the common Preface and Sanctus. The Canon is cut down to one prayer, a recast
*Quam oblationem* that flows into *Qui pridie*. The rubric in front of it says: "Only now
does the evangelical Mass begin".

### 2.1 Address, absolution and the recast *Orate fratres*

> *Zum ersten soll der priester oder ein anderer ein ermanung tun von dem sacrament oder
> sunst etwas tröstlichs us dem heiligen evangelio sagen, wie im der Geist Gottes eingibt
> etc. Darnach mag er die beisitzer mit solchen oder andern worten anreden:*
> Allerliebsten in Christo! Uf das ir mit frölichem, begirigem herzen entpfahent den
> zarten fronleichnam und rosenfarbes blut unsers seligmachers Jesu Christi, so demütigent
> eure herzen gegen Gott, bekennent im eure sünd und gebrechen mit herzlicher begir und
> sänen nach seiner göttlichen gnad und hilf!
> So will ich jetzt, euer erwölter priester, euch mitteilen die heiligen absolution und
> Gott für euch bitten.
> Der allmechtig, barmherzig Gott vergeb euch euer sünd! Und ich, us bevelch unsers Herren
> Jesu Christi an statt der heiligen kirchen, sag euch frei, ledig und los aller euer
> sünden in dem namen des Vaters und Suns und des Heiligen Geists. Amen.
> Dieweil ich aber auch beschwert bin mit mancherlei gebrechen, so bitten Gott treulich für
> mich, das ich disen seinen dienst im zu lobe, euch und mir zu trost und heil möge fleißig
> usrichten und volenden.
> Psalm 123: Unser hilf soll sein in dem namen des Herren, der himmel und erden erschaffen
> hat.

*First the priest, or another, shall make an exhortation concerning the sacrament, or else
say somewhat comfortable out of the holy Gospel, as the Spirit of God giveth it him, etc.
Thereafter he may address them that sit by with such or other words:*
Dearly beloved in Christ! That ye may with joyful and desirous hearts receive the tender
body and rose-coloured blood of our Saviour Jesus Christ, humble your hearts before God,
confess unto him your sins and infirmities with hearty desire, and long after his divine
grace and help!
So will I now, your chosen priest, impart unto you the holy absolution, and pray God for
you.
The almighty and merciful God forgive you your sins! And I, by the commandment of our Lord
Jesus Christ, in the stead of the holy Church, pronounce you free, quit and loosed of all
your sins, in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost. Amen.
But forasmuch as I also am burdened with manifold infirmities, pray God faithfully for me,
that I may diligently perform and finish this his service to his praise, to your comfort
and mine, and to our salvation.
Psalm 123: Our help shall be in the name of the Lord, who hath made heaven and earth.

**Note.**

- **The absolution.** Sehling's note: it is put together from the *Indulgentiam* at the
  beginning of the Mass and the absolution of private confession.
- **The request for prayer.** "Dieweil ich aber auch beschwert bin … so bitten Gott
  treulich für mich" is, in Sehling's words, a "free recasting of the *Orate, fratres*".
  The Roman request is *ut meum ac vestrum sacrificium acceptabile fiat*. Here the priest
  asks for help to perform "this his service".
- **The versicle.** *Adiutorium nostrum in nomine Domini* (Ps 124:8, numbered 123 in the
  Vulgate) belongs to the prayers at the foot of the altar.
- **The 1524 edition** drops the lay minister ("oder ein anderer") and the psalm verse, and
  heads the absolution "Die absolution".

### 2.2 *Veni Sancte Spiritus* and collect (in place of *Veni sanctificator*)

> *Darnach soll der priester niderknien und sagen:*
> Nu laßt uns beten; sprechend alle heimlich mit mir:
> Kumm, Heiliger Geist, erfülle die herzen deiner gläubigen und entzind in inen das feur
> deiner göttlichen liebe, der du durch manigfaltigkeit der zungen die völker der ganzen
> welt versamlet hast in einigkeit des glaubens. Alleluja.
> O Herr, allmechtiger Gott, hilf, das bei uns sei und wone dein Heiliger Geist, das er
> uns erleuchte und lern alle warheit, beschütze und sterke in aller widerwertigkeit, durch
> Christum, unsern Herren! Amen.

*Thereafter the priest shall kneel down and say:*
Now let us pray; say ye all secretly with me:
Come, Holy Ghost, fill the hearts of thy faithful, and kindle in them the fire of thy
divine love, who through the diversity of tongues hast gathered the peoples of the whole
world in the unity of the faith. Alleluia.
O Lord, almighty God, grant that thy Holy Ghost may be and dwell with us, that he may
enlighten us and teach us all truth, and defend and strengthen us in all adversity;
through Christ our Lord! Amen.

**Note.**

- **The antiphon.** This is the Whitsun Vigil antiphon *Veni, Sancte Spiritus, reple
  tuorum corda fidelium*. Sehling notes that the diocese of Augsburg prayed it in the Mass
  in place of the offertory prayer *Veni, sanctificator*. The rubric keeps the posture of
  the offertory prayers (kneeling, "heimlich") but has the whole congregation say them.
- **The collect.** This is not a Roman offertory prayer. It resembles the Whitsuntide
  collect *Adsit nobis, quaesumus, Domine, virtus Spiritus Sancti … et ab omnibus tueatur
  adversis*, with "lern alle warheit" from John 16:13. The same collect opens the Bremen
  (§9) and Kiel (§11) Masses.

### 2.3 Preface and Sanctus

> *Prefation oder vorred der meß:*
> Erhebt euer herzen zu Gott und laßt uns danksagen Gott unserm Herren; denn das ist
> billich und recht! Ja, warlich ist es billich und recht, auch heilsam, das wir an aller
> orten und zu aller zeit dir, Herr, heiliger Vater, allmechtiger, ewiger Gott, danksagen
> durch Christum, unsern Herren, durch welchen alle engel und die himmelischen krefte
> anbeten, eeren und loben dein herrliche majestat. Mit denselbigen, bitten wir, wöllest
> zulassen und annemen unsere stimm, das wir dich auch loben und sprechen on ende:
> *Sanctus:*
> Heiliger, heiliger, heiliger Herre Gott Sabaoth; voll seind himmel und erd deiner
> herlichkeit. Osanna in den höchsten! Gebenedeit sei, der da kompt in dem namen des
> Herren. Glück und heil in den höchsten!

*The Preface, or foreword of the Mass:*
Lift up your hearts unto God, and let us give thanks unto God our Lord; for that is meet
and right! Yea, verily it is meet and right, and also wholesome, that we should in all
places and at all times give thanks unto thee, O Lord, holy Father, almighty, everlasting
God, through Christ our Lord, through whom all angels and the heavenly powers worship,
honour and praise thy glorious majesty. With the same, we beseech thee, vouchsafe to admit
and receive our voice, that we also may praise thee and say without end:
*Sanctus:*
Holy, holy, holy, Lord God of Sabaoth; heaven and earth are full of thy glory. Hosanna in
the highest! Blessed be he that cometh in the name of the Lord. Luck and salvation in the
highest!

**Note.**

- **The dialogue.** It is reduced to the priest's words, with the responses run in: *Sursum
  corda … Gratias agamus … Dignum et iustum est*.
- **The Preface.** This is the common Preface (*Vere dignum … per Christum Dominum nostrum.
  Per quem maiestatem tuam laudant Angeli, adorant Dominationes … cum quibus et nostras
  voces ut admitti iubeas deprecamur*). The ranks of angels are merged into "alle engel und
  die himmelischen krefte".
- **The Sanctus.** The second *Hosanna* is rendered "Glück und heil" (Ps 118:25, "O Lord,
  send now prosperity").

### 2.4 *Quam oblationem* and *Qui pridie*

> *Nun hebt sich erst die evangelisch meß an.*
> O allergütigister Vater, barmherziger, ewiger Gott, hilf, das dises brot und der wein
> uns werde und sei der warhaftig leib und das unschuldig blut deines allerliebsten Suns,
> unsers Herren Jesu Christi, welcher an dem tag vor seinem leiden nam das brot in seine
> heiligen hende, sah auf gen himmel zu dir, seinem allmechtigen Vater, saget dank, segnet
> und brach das brot und gab es seinen jüngern sprechende: Nemet hin und essent! Das ist
> mein leib, der für euch geben wirt.
> Desgleichen nach dem obentmal, nam er den kelch in sein heilige hend, danket, segnet und
> gab in seinen jüngern und sprach:
> Nement hin und trinkent alle daraus! Das ist der kelch des neuen und ewigen testaments in
> meinem blut, das für euch und für vil vergossen wirt zu vergebung der sünden. Als oft ir
> das tut, so tuts in meiner gedechtnis!

*Here beginneth first the evangelical Mass.*
O most gracious Father, merciful, everlasting God, grant that this bread and the wine may
become and be unto us the true body and the innocent blood of thy most dear Son, our Lord
Jesus Christ; who on the day before his passion took the bread into his holy hands, looked
up to heaven unto thee, his almighty Father, gave thanks, blessed, and brake the bread, and
gave it to his disciples, saying: Take, and eat! This is my body, which is given for you.
Likewise after the supper he took the cup into his holy hands, gave thanks, blessed, and
gave it to his disciples, and said:
Take, and drink ye all of it! This is the cup of the new and everlasting testament in my
blood, which is shed for you and for many for the remission of sins. As oft as ye do this,
do it in remembrance of me!

**Note.**

- **The rubric.** "Nun hebt sich erst die evangelisch meß an": everything before is
  preparation. The Canon proper begins here, with the one Canon prayer that is kept.
- **The recast *Quam oblationem*.** Sehling calls it "the prayer after the conclusion of the
  Canon prayer *Quam oblationem*". The Roman text is *Quam oblationem tu, Deus, in omnibus,
  quaesumus, benedictam, adscriptam, ratam, rationabilem acceptabilemque facere digneris:
  ut nobis Corpus et Sanguis fiat dilectissimi Filii tui Domini nostri Iesu Christi*. Kantz
  keeps the petition *ut nobis … fiat* and the superlative *dilectissimi*. He drops the
  oblation and its five adjectives. The object becomes "dises brot und der wein".
- ***Qui pridie*** keeps *elevatis oculis in caelum ad te Deum Patrem suum omnipotentem*
  ("sah auf gen himmel zu dir, seinem allmechtigen Vater"), both *benedixit*, and *novi et
  aeterni testamenti*. It drops *mysterium fidei*.
  - "für euch und für vil" is *pro vobis et pro multis*.
  - "Als oft ir das tut, so tuts in meiner gedechtnis" is *Haec quotiescumque feceritis, in
    mei memoriam facietis*.
- **The 1524 edition** has "nachtmal" for "obentmal", and "in sein eerwirdigen hende, saget
  aber dank, segnet ihn" (*venerabiles manus*).

### 2.5 Our Father with the conclusion of the embolism; *Agnus Dei*; *Fili Dei vivi*

> Laßt uns beten:
> Vater unser, der du bist im himmel, geheiliget werd dein nam! Zukumm dein reich! Dein
> will der werd als im himmel und auf der erd! Unser täglich brot gib uns heut und vergib
> uns unser schuld, als wir vergeben unsern schuldigern! Und für uns nit in versuchung,
> sonder erlöse uns von übel! Durch unsern Herren Jesum, deinen Sun, welcher mit dir und
> dem Heiligen Geist, ein warer Gott, lebt und herrscht in ewigkeit. Amen.
> *Agnus Dei:*
> O du lamb Gottes, der du tregst die sünd der welt, erbarm dich unser und gib uns deinen
> frid! Amen.
> *Ein andechtigs gebet vor der entpfahung des sakraments:*
> O Herr Jesu Christe, du ewigs wort des Vaters, du Heiland der welt, du warer, lebendiger
> Gott und mensch, erlös uns durch disen deinen heiligen fronleichnam und rosenvarbes blut
> von allen sünden! Hilf, das wir erfüllen deine gebot zu aller zeit und von dir nit
> gescheiden werden in ewigkeit! Amen.

Let us pray:
Our Father, which art in heaven, hallowed be thy name! Thy kingdom come! Thy will be done,
as in heaven, so in earth! Give us this day our daily bread, and forgive us our debts, as
we forgive our debtors! And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil! Through
our Lord Jesus, thy Son, who with thee and the Holy Ghost, one true God, liveth and
reigneth for ever. Amen.
*Agnus Dei:*
O thou Lamb of God, that bearest the sin of the world, have mercy upon us and give us thy
peace! Amen.
*A devout prayer before the receiving of the sacrament:*
O Lord Jesu Christ, thou everlasting Word of the Father, thou Saviour of the world, thou
true, living God and man, deliver us by this thy holy body and rose-coloured blood from all
sins! Help us that we may fulfil thy commandments at all times, and never be parted from
thee for ever! Amen.

**Note.**

- **The end of the embolism.** As at Worms (§6.10), the Our Father runs straight into the
  conclusion of *Libera nos, quaesumus* ("Per eundem Dominum nostrum Iesum Christum,
  Filium tuum, qui tecum vivit et regnat in unitate Spiritus Sancti Deus"). The body of the
  embolism is gone.
- **The fraction, in the 1524 edition only.** The 1524 edition adds the doxology "denn dein
  ist das reich und die kraft und die herligkeit in ewigkeit. Amen." It then adds a rubric
  before the conclusion:

  > Hie neme der priester das brot und breche es, so vil ers bedarf, und sprech:

  *Here let the priest take the bread and break it, as much as he needeth, and say:*

  - **Where the rubric falls.** Sehling's note 7 points out that the Roman fraction came at
    these same words, at the end of the embolism. There the priest broke the host in
    three and dropped one piece into the chalice. In 1524 the conclusion "Durch unsern
    Herren …" stands isolated after the doxology's *Amen*, so the breaking marks the old
    place.
  - **Was it new in 1524?** Sehling judges (notes 7–9) that the 1522 edition did not know
    the fraction. There the priest shows "ein hostien", any host, while in 1524 he shows
    "die", the host just broken.
  - **No commixture.** Neither edition drops a piece into the cup.
- **The *Agnus Dei*.** It is sung once, with *dona nobis pacem*.
- ***Fili Dei vivi*.** The prayer before communion is an abridged *Domine Iesu Christe,
  Fili Dei vivi … libera me per hoc sacrosanctum Corpus et Sanguinem tuum ab omnibus
  iniquitatibus meis et universis malis: et fac me tuis semper inhaerere mandatis, et a te
  numquam separari permittas*.
  - The opening clause (*qui ex voluntate Patris …*) gives way to a string of titles.
  - The prayer is plural throughout.
  - The 1524 edition heads it "Oratio".

### 2.6 Communion, with the *custodiat* and *Calicem salutaris*

> *Nach disem gebet entpfahe der priester das sakrament, ist er anders geschickt darzu,
> und nem darnach ein hostien in die hand, zeige oder weise die den communicanten und
> sprech also:*
> Secht, allerliebsten, das ist warlich der heilig leichnam unsers Herren Jesu Christi, der
> für euch gelitten hat den bittern tod. Nement hin und essent in, das er euch speis, neer
> und beware in das ewig leben! Amen. Der frid sei mit euch allen!
> *So er sie nu alle communiciert hat mit dem heiligen fronleichnam Christi, sprech er
> also:*
> Laßt uns auch trinken den kelch des heils und anrufen den namen unsers Herren!
> *Und wenn er getrunken hat, soll er sich mit dem kelch oder becher zu inen keren und also
> sprechen:*
> Secht, das ist warlich der teur schatz des kostbarlichen bluts unsers Herren Jesu
> Christi, damit ir erkauft seit. Nement hin und teilents miteinander zu abwäschung euer
> sünden!

*After this prayer let the priest receive the sacrament, if he be otherwise prepared
thereto, and then take a host in his hand, show or point it to the communicants, and say
thus:*
Behold, dearly beloved, this is verily the holy body of our Lord Jesus Christ, which
suffered for you the bitter death. Take and eat it, that it may feed, nourish and preserve
you unto everlasting life! Amen. Peace be with you all!
*When he hath now communicated them all with the holy body of Christ, let him say thus:*
Let us also drink the cup of salvation and call upon the name of our Lord!
*And when he hath drunk, he shall turn him to them with the chalice or cup, and say thus:*
Behold, this is verily the precious treasure of the costly blood of our Lord Jesus Christ,
wherewith ye are bought. Take it, and divide it among you, to the washing away of your
sins!

**Note.**

- **"Secht …".** The showing of the host with "Behold" takes the place of the elevation
  (Sehling: "Die Elevation, die Kantz also vom Konsekrationsakt in die Kommunion verlegt
  hat"). It also recalls the *Ecce Agnus Dei* of the late-medieval communion of the people.
- **"… das er euch speis, neer und beware in das ewig leben"** is the *custodiat* formula
  (*Corpus Domini nostri Iesu Christi custodiat animam tuam in vitam aeternam*), spoken to
  all at once.
- **"Der frid sei mit euch allen".** The *Pax Domini* has moved from before the *Agnus
  Dei* to the communion. The 1524 edition makes it "Der frid sei mit dir!", said to each
  communicant.
- **"Laßt uns auch trinken den kelch des heils …"** is the priest's *Calicem salutaris
  accipiam, et nomen Domini invocabo* (Ps 116:13), from his prayers at the sumption. It
  is turned into a plural invitation.
- **The cup.** "teilents miteinander" echoes Luke 22:17.

### 2.7 Thanksgiving: *Nunc dimittis*, *Te Deum*, collects

> *So das geschehen ist, soll der priester niderknien und danksagen:*
> O Herr, nu laß im frid deine diener nach deinem wort; denn unsere augen haben gesehen
> dein Heiland, welchen du bereitet hast vor dem angesicht aller völker, ein liecht zu
> erleüchten die heiden und zu einer glori deins volks Israel. Dir sei lob, eer und dank, o
> du heilige, gebenedeite, herrliche dreivaltigkeit, Gott Vater, Sun und Heiliger Geist!
> Amen.
> *Item zu danksagung mag man sprechen:*
> Te Deum laudamus. Gott, dich loben wir; dich, Herr, bekennen wir. […]
> Nunc dimittis servum tuum, Domine, secundum verbum tuum in pace […]. Gloria Patri etc.,
> sicut erat in principio et nunc et semper et in secula seculorum. Amen.
> O gütiger, barmherziger Got, ein sterk aller, die in dich hoffen, hilf, das wir also
> wandlen durch das zeitlich, uf das wir nit verlieren das ewig, durch Christum, unsern
> Herren. Amen.
> O du heilige dreivaltigkeit, laß dir wolgefallen unser lob und danksagung! Mach vest und
> stet das werk, das du in uns verbracht[!] hast! Hilf, das uns fruchtbar und fürderlich
> sei in das ewig leben! Amen.

*When that is done, the priest shall kneel down and give thanks:*
O Lord, now lettest thou thy servants depart in peace according to thy word; for our eyes
have seen thy salvation, which thou hast prepared before the face of all peoples, a light
to lighten the Gentiles, and to be the glory of thy people Israel. Unto thee be praise,
honour and thanks, O thou holy, blessed, glorious Trinity, God the Father, Son and Holy
Ghost! Amen.
*Item, for thanksgiving one may say:*
Te Deum laudamus. We praise thee, O God; we acknowledge thee to be the Lord. […]
Lord, now lettest thou thy servant depart in peace, according to thy word […]. Glory be to
the Father, etc., as it was in the beginning, is now, and ever shall be, world without end.
Amen.
O gracious and merciful God, the strength of all that hope in thee, help us so to walk
through things temporal that we lose not the things eternal; through Christ our Lord.
Amen.
O thou holy Trinity, let our praise and thanksgiving be well-pleasing unto thee! Make firm
and steadfast the work which thou hast wrought in us! Grant that it may be fruitful and
profitable unto us unto everlasting life! Amen.

**Note.**

- **The *Nunc dimittis*.** Made plural ("deine diener", "unsere augen"). Sehling notes it
  was among the priest's silent prayers at this point in some German churches.
- **The first collect.** Sehling identifies it as the collect of the third Sunday after
  Pentecost, *Protector in te sperantium, Deus … ut sic transeamus per bona temporalia, ut
  non amittamus aeterna*, not a postcommunion.
- **The Trinity prayer is the *Placeat*.** Sehling thought it "wohl von Kantz geschaffen".
  It is in fact the priest's last prayer of the Roman Mass, said before the blessing:
  *Placeat tibi, sancta Trinitas, obsequium servitutis meae: et praesta, ut sacrificium,
  quod oculis tuae maiestatis indignus obtuli, tibi sit acceptabile, mihique et omnibus,
  pro quibus illud obtuli, sit, te miserante, propitiabile*.
  - "laß dir wolgefallen unser lob und danksagung" is *placeat tibi … obsequium*.
  - The sacrifice offered becomes "das werk, das du in uns verbracht hast": God's work in
    us, not ours before him.
  - "fruchtbar und fürderlich … in das ewig leben" takes the place of *propitiabile*.
- **What follows.** The order ends by permitting the Latin *Te Deum* and the Magnificat
  "for thanksgiving". There is no blessing.

---

## 3. Wittenberg, Luther, *Formula missae et communionis*, 1523

Sehling 1, Martin Luther's Ordnungen Nr. 4, pp. 4–9 (`eko.db` doc 2; the database dates
the document 1889, the year of Sehling's volume). The order is in Latin. It is included
because it is the model the German Masses answer to. Brandenburg 1540 (§12) takes its Latin
communion prayers from the permissions given here. The Nürnberg Latin order of 1524 (§7.1)
takes its shortened Preface from it.

Luther states the rule first: everything that "sounds of oblation", with the whole Canon,
is to go. What is "pure and holy" stays. The paragraphs below are numbered as in the
print.

### 3.1 The rule, and the preparation of bread and wine

> Octavo, sequitur tota illa abominatio, cui servire coactum est quicquid in missa
> praecessit, unde et offertorium vocatur. Et abhinc omnia fere sonant ac olent
> oblationem. […] Proinde omnibus illis repudiatis quae oblationem sonant, cum universo
> canone, retineamus, quae pura et sancta sunt, ac sic missam nostram ordiamur.
> I. Sub symbolo vel post canonem apparetur panis et vinum ad benedictionem ritu solito,
> nisi quod nondum constitui mecum, miscendane sit aqua vino, quamquam huc inclino, ut
> merum potius vinum paretur absque aquae mixtura […]. Tamen contra libertatem non
> introducam legem superstitiosam.

Eighthly, there followeth all that abomination, to which everything that went before in
the Mass was forced to serve, whence also it is called the offertory. And from here on
almost everything soundeth and savoureth of oblation. […] Therefore, all those things being
rejected which sound of oblation, together with the whole Canon, let us retain those things
which are pure and holy, and so begin our Mass.
I. During the Creed, or after the canon [*sermon*], let bread and wine be made ready for the blessing
in the accustomed manner; save that I have not yet determined with myself whether water be
to be mixed with the wine, though I incline to this, that pure wine rather be prepared
without the mixture of water […]. Yet I will not bring in a superstitious law against
liberty.

**Note.**

- **"post canonem".** This is Sehling's reading. The Weimar edition (WA 12, 211) reads
  *post concionem*, "after the sermon". That fits the paragraph before, which lets the
  vernacular sermon come after the Creed.
- **What goes.** The offertory prayers (*Suscipe, sancte Pater*; *Offerimus tibi*; *Veni,
  sanctificator*; *Suscipe, sancta Trinitas*), the *Orate fratres*, the Secret and the
  whole Canon, as all "sounding of oblation".
- **What stays** is set out in §§II–VIII below.

### 3.2 Preface, Verba, Sanctus with elevation

> II. Apparato pane et vino mox procedatur ad hunc modum: 'Dominus vobiscum', respon.:
> 'Et cum spiritu tuo'. 'Sursum corda', respon.: 'Habeamus ad dominum'. 'Gratias agamus
> domino deo nostro'. Respon.: 'dignum et iustum est. Vere dignum et iustum est, equum et
> salutare, nos tibi semper et ubique gratias agere, domine sancte, pater omnipotens,
> aeterne deus, per Christum dominum nostrum'.
> III. Deinde: 'Qui pridie quam pateretur, accepit panem gratias agens, fregit deditque
> discipulis suis dicens: Accipite, comedite, hoc est corpus meum, quod pro vobis datur.
> Similiter et calicem, postquam caenavit, dicens: Hic calix est novi testamenti in meo
> sanguine, qui pro vobis et pro multis effundetur in remissionem peccatorum. Haec
> quotiescunque feceritis, in mei memoriam faciatis.'
> Haec verba Christi velim modica post prefationem interposita pausa in eo tono vocis
> recitari, quo canitur alias oratio dominica in canone, ut a circumstantibus possit
> audiri, quamquam in his omnibus libertas sit piis mentibus, vel silenter vel palam ea
> verba recitare.
> IV. Finita benedictione Chorus cantet Sanctus et sub cantu Benedictus elevetur panis et
> calix, ritu hactenus servato, vel propter infirmos, qui hac repentina huius insignioris
> in missa ritus [mutatione] forte offendentur, praesertim ubi per conciones vernaculas
> docti fuerint, quid ea petatur elevatione.

II. The bread and wine being made ready, let him straightway proceed after this manner:
"The Lord be with you"; answer, "And with thy spirit". "Lift up your hearts"; answer, "Let
us lift them up unto the Lord". "Let us give thanks unto the Lord our God". Answer: "It is
meet and right. It is very meet and right, fitting and wholesome, that we should at all
times and in all places give thanks unto thee, O Lord, holy Father, almighty, everlasting
God, through Christ our Lord".
III. Then: "Who the day before he suffered took bread, giving thanks, brake it, and gave it
to his disciples, saying: Take, eat; this is my body, which is given for you. Likewise also
the cup, after he had supped, saying: This cup is the new testament in my blood, which
shall be shed for you and for many for the remission of sins. As oft as ye shall do these
things, ye shall do them in remembrance of me."
These words of Christ I would have recited, after a short pause interposed after the
Preface, in that tone of voice in which the Lord's Prayer is otherwise sung in the Canon,
so that they may be heard by them that stand about; although in all these things let
godly minds have liberty to recite those words either silently or aloud.
IV. The blessing being ended, let the choir sing the Sanctus, and during the singing of the
Benedictus let the bread and cup be elevated after the rite hitherto kept, chiefly for the
sake of the weak, who might perhaps be offended by a sudden change of this more notable
rite in the Mass, especially where they have been taught by sermons in the vulgar tongue
what is sought by that elevation.

**Note.**

- **The Preface.** It is cut off after *per Christum Dominum nostrum*. The Verba follow as a
  relative clause on *Christum* ("Qui pridie …"). This is the form Nürnberg took up in 1524
  (§7.1) and the German orders of Volprecht (§7.2) and Döber (§8.2) translated.
- ***Qui pridie*** is reduced to the Gospel and Pauline wording.
  - Gone are *in sanctas ac venerabiles manus suas*, *elevatis oculis*, *benedixit*,
    *aeterni* and *mysterium fidei*.
  - "quod pro vobis datur" (Luke 22:19) and "hic calix est novi testamenti in meo sanguine"
    (1 Cor 11:25) come from Scripture, not the Missal.
- **"benedictio".** Luther calls the recitation of the Verba the "benedictio".
- **The Sanctus and elevation.** The Sanctus, which in the Roman Mass comes before the
  Canon, is moved after the Verba. The elevation is kept "ritu hactenus servato", during
  the *Benedictus*.

### 3.3 Our Father; no embolism, no fraction; the Peace as absolution

> V. Post haec legatur oratio dominica. Sic: 'Oremus. Preceptis salutaribus moniti etc.'
> omissa oratione sequenti: 'Libera nos quesumus', cum omnibus signis, quae fieri solent
> super hostiam et cum hostia super calicem, nec frangatur hostia nec in calicem misceatur.
> Sed statim post orationem dominicam dicatur: 'Pax domini etc.' quae est publica quaedam
> absolutio a peccatis communicantium, vox plane evangelica, annuncians remissionem
> peccatorum, unica illa et dignissima ad mensam domini preparatio, si fide apprehendatur,
> non secus atque ex ore Christi prolata. Unde vellem eam nunciari verso ad populum vultu,
> quemadmodum solent episcopi, quod unicum est vestigium episcoporum priscorum in nostris
> episcopis.

V. After these things let the Lord's Prayer be read, thus: "Let us pray. Taught by
wholesome precepts, etc.", the prayer following, "Deliver us, we beseech thee", being
omitted, with all the signs that are wont to be made over the host and with the host over
the chalice; and let the host not be broken, nor mixed into the chalice. But straightway
after the Lord's Prayer let there be said "The peace of the Lord, etc.", which is a kind of
public absolution from the sins of the communicants, a voice plainly evangelical,
announcing remission of sins, that one and most worthy preparation for the Lord's table,
if it be laid hold on by faith, no otherwise than as spoken from the mouth of Christ.
Wherefore I would have it announced with the face turned to the people, as bishops are
wont to do, which is the only trace of the ancient bishops left in our bishops.

**Note.**

- **The embolism** (*Libera nos, quaesumus, Domine, ab omnibus malis …*) is struck out,
  with its signs of the cross made over the host and the chalice with a fragment.
- **The fraction and commixture are forbidden.** "nec frangatur hostia nec in calicem
  misceatur". Worms 1524 repeats this in German (§6.11). Of the Lutheran orders in the
  corpus, only Kantz's second edition (1524, §2.5) and Müntzer's Allstedt order (§4.2)
  break the bread at this point.
- **The Peace.** *Pax Domini sit semper vobiscum* is reinterpreted as a public absolution.
  Worms and Brandenburg keep it in that sense.

### 3.4 Communion: *Agnus Dei*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *custodiat*

> VI. Deinde communicet tum sese, tum populum, interim cantetur Agnus dei. Quod si
> orationem illam: 'Domine Ihesu Christe, fili dei vivi, qui ex voluntate patris etc.' ante
> sumptionem orare voluerit, non male orabit, mutato solum numero singulari in pluralem,
> 'nostris' et 'nos' pro 'meis' et 'me'. Item et illam: 'Corpus domini etc. custodiat
> animam meam, vel tuam, in vitam aeternam'. Et 'sanguis domini nostri custodiat animam
> tuam in vitam aeternam'.

VI. Then let him communicate first himself, then the people; meanwhile let the Agnus Dei be
sung. But if he will pray before the sumption that prayer, "O Lord Jesus Christ, Son of the
living God, who by the will of the Father, etc.", he shall not pray amiss, only changing
the singular number into the plural, "our" and "us" for "my" and "me". Likewise also that
other: "The body of the Lord, etc., preserve my soul, or thy soul, unto life eternal". And
"The blood of our Lord preserve thy soul unto life eternal".

**Note.**

- **What is permitted.** Of the three prayers before the priest's communion, only
  *Domine Iesu Christe, Fili Dei vivi* is allowed, and it is made plural. *Domine Iesu
  Christe, qui dixisti* and *Perceptio corporis* are not mentioned. Brandenburg 1540
  nevertheless kept all three (§12.5).
- **The *custodiat*.** It serves both for the priest ("meam") and for each communicant
  ("tuam").
- **The *Agnus Dei*.** It is sung during communion, not before it.

### 3.5 Communion chant; *Quod ore sumpsimus* and *Corpus tuum* for the postcommunion;
*Benedicamus*; blessing

> VII. Si communionem cantare libet, cantetur. Sed loco complendae seu ultimae collectae,
> quia fere sacrificium sonant, legatur in eodem tono oratio illa: 'Quod ore sumpsimus,
> domine'. Poterit et illa legi: 'Corpus tuum, domine, quod sumpsimus etc.' mutato numero
> in pluralem. 'Qui vivis et regnas etc.' 'Dominus vobiscum etc.' Loco 'Ite missa' dicatur:
> 'Benedicamus domino', adiecto (ubi et quando placet) alleluia in suis melodiis. Vel ex
> vespertinis 'Benedicamus' mutuentur.
> VIII. Benedictio solita detur. Vel accipiatur illa Numeri 6. quam ipse dominus digessit,
> d.: 'Benedicat nos dominus et custodiat nos, ostendat nobis faciem suam et misereatur
> nostri, convertat dominus faciem suam ad nos et det nobis pacem.' Vel illa psal. 96.:
> 'Benedicat nos deus deus noster, benedicat nos deus et metuant eum omnes fines terrae.
> Amen.'

VII. If it please them to sing the communion, let it be sung. But in the place of the
complenda or last collect, because they almost all sound of sacrifice, let that prayer be
read in the same tone: "What we have taken with our mouth, O Lord". That other also may be
read: "Thy body, O Lord, which we have received, etc.", the number being changed into the
plural. "Who livest and reignest, etc." "The Lord be with you, etc." In the place of "Ite,
missa est" let there be said "Let us bless the Lord", with Alleluia added (where and when it
pleaseth) in its own melodies; or let the Benedicamus be borrowed from Vespers.
VIII. Let the accustomed blessing be given. Or let that of Numbers 6 be taken, which the
Lord himself appointed, saying: "The Lord bless us and keep us, show us his face and have
mercy upon us; the Lord turn his face unto us and give us peace." Or that of Psalm 96
[67]: "God, even our own God, bless us; God bless us, and let all the ends of the earth
fear him. Amen."

**Note.**

- **The postcommunion.** The variable postcommunions (*complenda*) are rejected because
  "they almost all sound of sacrifice". The two priest's prayers said at the ablutions
  take their place, read aloud in the collect tone:
  - *Quod ore sumpsimus, Domine, pura mente capiamus, et de munere temporali fiat nobis
    remedium sempiternum*;
  - *Corpus tuum, Domine, quod sumpsi, et Sanguis, quem potavi, adhaereat visceribus meis
    …*, made plural.

  Brandenburg 1540 and Pfalz-Neuburg 1543 kept both in Latin (§12.8, §14.8). Volprecht
  (§7.2) and Strasbourg (§5.1) turned *Quod ore* into German.
- ***Benedicamus Domino*** replaces *Ite, missa est*.
- **The blessing.** Luther's use of Numbers 6 is the origin of the Aaronic blessing at the
  end of the Lutheran Mass.

### 3.6 The *Deutsche Messe* of 1526 (for comparison)

Luther's German Mass (Sehling 1, Nr. 8; doc 3) keeps none of the Canon or of the priest's
prayers:

- **In place of the Canon** stands a public paraphrase of the Our Father, with an
  admonition to the communicants: "Nach der predigt sol folgen eine offentliche
  paraphrasis des vater unsers und vermanung an die so zum sacrament gehen wollen …".
- **The Verba.** Luther suggested giving the bread straight after the bread-word, "ehe man
  den kilch segenet", and the cup after the cup-word.
- **The elevation** is kept: "Das aufheben wollen wir nicht abthun sondern behalten,
  darumb das es fein mit dem deudschen sanctus stimmet …".
- **No fraction, no *Pax*.**
- **The thanksgiving** is a new collect, "Wir dancken dir, almechtiger herre gott, das du
  uns durch dise heilsame gabe hast erquicket …". Bugenhagen's orders (§15) and Brandenburg
  1540 (§12.8) carry it.

This is why most Lutheran orders after 1526 have no Canon remnants. The orders in this file
are those that, like the *Formula missae*, kept something of the old text.

---

## 4. Allstedt, Thomas Müntzer, *Deutsch evangelisch Messe*, 1524, and *Ordnung des teutschen ampts*, 1523/24

- **The Mass.** *Deutsch evangelisch messze, etwan durch die Bebstischen pfaffen im latein
  zu grossem nachteil des Christen glaubens vor ein opffer gehandelt …* Sehling 1, Nr. 46,
  pp. 497–503 (`eko.db` doc 51). It gives five Masses (Advent, Christmas, Passion, Easter,
  Whitsun) with their propers set to the old chant. The ordinary is printed in full with
  the first, the Advent Mass, on pp. 500–501. It is given here.
- **The commentary.** *Ordnung und berechnunge des teutschen ampts zu Alstadt*, 1523,
  printed at Allstedt 1524. Sehling 1, Nr. 47, pp. 504–506 (doc 52). It explains the Mass
  item by item and gives the rubrics the Mass itself lacks.

Müntzer was no Lutheran, but his German Mass is the most conservative in the corpus in
wording. It keeps:

- the offertory chant, with the Secret's *Per omnia saecula saeculorum*;
- the full proper Preface;
- the Roman *Qui pridie* with *mysterium fidei*;
- the end of the embolism, with the fraction (per the *Ordnung*);
- *Pax Domini*.

His wording was taken over in the Lippe Mass (§10), the Kiel Low German Mass (§11) and,
for the Preface dialogue, Calenberg-Göttingen 1542 (§13).

### 4.1 Offertory, Preface, Sanctus (Advent)

> O herr, zu dir hab ich erhaben meine sele; o gott, mein gott, auf dich vorlass ich mich
> und werd nit zu schanden, das mich meine feinde nicht bespotten, durch alle ewigkeit der
> ewigkeit. Amen. Der herre sei mit euch. Und mit deinem geiste. Unser herzen in die höe.
> Haben wir zu dem herren. Wir sagen dank dem herren, unserem gotte. Es ist wirdig und ist
> recht. Warlich, es ist billich und recht und ist heilsam, das wir dir, herr, o heiliger
> vater, almechtiger, ewiger got, allzeit und allenthalben danksagen. Dann du dein heilige
> menscheit von der junkfrauen Maria hast empfangen durch die umbschetigung des heilgen
> geistes, das sie mit unvorruckter keuscheit das ewige licht zur welt gebracht hat, Jesum
> Christum, unseren herren. Durch wilchen loben die engel dein herligkeit und ehr erbieten
> die engel, do du innen hirschest; es entsetzten sich die gewaltigen engel; dozu die
> himmel und der himmel krefte und die heiligen seraphin preisen dich on unterlass mit
> einmütiger freuden. Drumb bitten wir dich, o herr, das du woltest unsere stimmen mit in
> zu lassen, das wir dich mit warem bekentnis mügen loben one ende, sagende: Heilger,
> heilger, heilger herre gott Sabaoth. Himmel und erde seint erfullet mit deinem preise.
> Ozianna in dem höchsten. Gesegnet sei, der do kumpt im namen des herren. Ozianna in den
> höchsten.

O Lord, unto thee have I lifted up my soul; O God, my God, in thee I trust, and shall not
be put to shame, that mine enemies mock me not; world without end. Amen. The Lord be with
you. And with thy spirit. Our hearts on high. We have them unto the Lord. We give thanks
unto the Lord our God. It is worthy and it is right. Verily it is meet and right and it is
wholesome that we should at all times and in all places give thanks unto thee, O Lord,
holy Father, almighty, everlasting God. For thou hast received thy holy manhood of the
Virgin Mary by the overshadowing of the Holy Ghost, that she with unblemished chastity
brought forth unto the world the everlasting light, Jesus Christ our Lord. Through whom the
angels praise thy glory, and the angels in whom thou reignest do thee honour; the mighty
angels tremble; and the heavens and the powers of the heavens and the holy seraphim praise
thee without ceasing with one accord of joy. Therefore we beseech thee, O Lord, that thou
wouldest let our voices be admitted with them, that we may praise thee with true
confession without end, saying: Holy, holy, holy, Lord God of Sabaoth. Heaven and earth are
filled with thy praise. Hosanna in the highest. Blessed be he that cometh in the name of
the Lord. Hosanna in the highest.

**Note.**

- **The offertory chant.** "O herr, zu dir hab ich erhaben meine sele …" is the offertory
  chant of the first Sunday of Advent, *Ad te, Domine, levavi animam meam: Deus meus, in
  te confido, non erubescam: neque irrideant me inimici mei* (Ps 25:1–2). The *Ordnung* says
  "Wir halten kein opfer in der geheim gotis": the offertory prayers are gone, but not the
  chant.
- **"durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit. Amen."** This is *Per omnia saecula saeculorum.
  Amen*, the audible end of the silent Secret, which led into the Preface dialogue. The
  Secret itself is gone; its last words remain. The same place in Lippe (§10) has it as the
  first words of the "Prefatio", and Göttingen 1542 (§13) has "Gott sey preis von
  ewigkeit zu ewigkeit. Amen."
- **The Preface** is the Preface of the Blessed Virgin, not the Advent or Christmas
  Preface. Its text is *Et te in [festivitate] beatae Mariae semper
  Virginis collaudare … Quae et Unigenitum tuum Sancti Spiritus obumbratione concepit: et
  virginitatis gloria permanente, lumen aeternum mundo effudit, Iesum Christum Dominum
  nostrum. Per quem maiestatem tuam laudant Angeli, adorant Dominationes, tremunt
  Potestates. Caeli caelorumque Virtutes, ac beata Seraphim, socia exsultatione
  concelebrant*.
  - Müntzer takes out the praise of Mary. God himself becomes the subject: "du [hast] dein
    heilige menscheit … empfangen".
  - The Virgin's *virginitatis gloria permanente* becomes "mit unvorruckter keuscheit".
  - The ranks of angels are rendered one by one: *Dominationes* "die engel, do du innen
    hirschest"; *tremunt Potestates* "es entsetzten sich die gewaltigen engel".
- **"mit warem bekentnis"** is *supplici confessione*.

### 4.2 The Verba, the Our Father, the end of the embolism, the Peace, *Agnus Dei*

> Einen tag zuvorn, do Jesus wolte leiden, nam er das brot in seine heiligen hende und hub
> auf seine augen in himmel zu dir, gott seinem almechtigen vater, und sagte dir dank und
> gesegnete es und brach es und gab es seinen jüngern, sagende: Nempt hin und esset
> darvon. Das ist mein leichnam, der vor euch dargegeben wirt. Des selbigen gleichen, do
> man gessen hatte, nam er den kelch in seine heiligen wirdigen hende und sagete dir dank
> und gesegnete den und gab in seinen jüngern, sagende: Nempt hin und trinket all daraus.
> Dis ist der kilch meines blutes des neuen und ewigen bundes, ein geheimnis des glaubens,
> der vor euch und vor viel vorgossen wirt in vorgebunge der sunde. So oft ir dis thut,
> solt ir meiner dobei gedenken. Dorumb last uns alle bitten, wie uns Jesus Christus, der
> sohn gotes, hat geleret, sagende: Vater unser, der du bist in himmeln, geheiliget werde
> dein name, zu kum dein reich, dein will geschee als im himmel und auf erden. Unser
> teglich brot gib uns heute und vorlass uns unsere schulde, wie wir vorlassen unsern
> schuldigern. Und fur uns nicht in vorsuchunge, sondern erlöse uns von übel. Amen. Durch
> alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit. Amen. Der fride des herren sei allzeit mit euch. Und mit
> deinem geist. O lamp gotes, wilchs do tregt die sunde der welt, erbarm dich unser. O
> lamp gotes, wilchs do tregt die sunde der welt, gib uns deinen fride.

The day before, when Jesus would suffer, he took the bread into his holy hands, and lifted
up his eyes to heaven unto thee, God, his almighty Father, and gave thanks unto thee, and
blessed it, and brake it, and gave it to his disciples, saying: Take, and eat thereof. This
is my body, which is given for you. In like manner, when they had eaten, he took the cup
into his holy and worthy hands, and gave thanks unto thee, and blessed it, and gave it to
his disciples, saying: Take, and drink ye all thereof. This is the cup of my blood of the
new and everlasting covenant, a mystery of faith, which is shed for you and for many for
the forgiveness of sins. As oft as ye do this, ye shall remember me thereby. Therefore let
us all pray, as Jesus Christ, the Son of God, hath taught us, saying: Our Father, which art
in the heavens, hallowed be thy name, thy kingdom come, thy will be done, as in heaven, so
on earth. Give us this day our daily bread, and forgive us our debts, as we forgive our
debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil. Amen. World without
end. Amen. The peace of the Lord be alway with you. And with thy spirit. O Lamb of God,
which beareth the sin of the world, have mercy upon us. O Lamb of God, which beareth the
sin of the world, give us thy peace.

**Note.**

- **The Verba are the Roman *Qui pridie*, not the Scriptural text.** "Einen tag zuvorn, do
  Jesus wolte leiden" is *Qui pridie quam pateretur*. The following are all kept:
  - *in sanctas ac venerabiles manus suas* ("in seine heiligen [wirdigen] hende");
  - *elevatis oculis in caelum ad te Deum Patrem suum omnipotentem* ("hub auf seine augen
    in himmel zu dir, gott seinem almechtigen vater");
  - *tibi gratias agens* ("sagte dir dank"), still addressed to the Father in the second
    person;
  - *benedixit* ("gesegnete es");
  - *Accipite et manducate ex hoc omnes* ("esset darvon");
  - *novi et aeterni testamenti* ("des neuen und ewigen bundes");
  - ***mysterium fidei*** ("ein geheimnis des glaubens");
  - *Haec quotiescumque feceritis, in mei memoriam facietis*.

  Only "der vor euch dargegeben wirt" (Luke 22:19) is added. The *Ordnung* (below) has the
  same text, with crosses at the two blessings.
- **"Dorumb last uns alle bitten, wie uns Jesus Christus … hat geleret, sagende"** is
  *Oremus: Praeceptis salutaribus moniti, et divina institutione formati, audemus
  dicere*.
- **"Durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit. Amen."** after the Our Father is *Per omnia
  saecula saeculorum. Amen*, the sung ending of the embolism *Libera nos*. The embolism is
  gone and its ending stays. The *Ordnung* puts the fraction here (§4.3).
- **"Der fride des herren sei allzeit mit euch. Und mit deinem geist"** is *Pax Domini sit
  semper vobiscum. Et cum spiritu tuo*, with the Roman response.
- **The *Agnus Dei*** is printed twice in the Mass and three times in the *Ordnung*.

### 4.3 The Allstedt *Ordnung* on the Sacrament: Verba, elevation, fraction

> Zum neunten: So singet man die form der termung oder des abentessens im tone der
> prefation, lauts diser wort folgende: Einen tag zuvorn, do Jesus wolte leiden, nam er
> das brot in seine heiligen, wirdigen hende, und hub auf seine augen in himmel zu dir,
> got, seinem almechtigen vater, sagete dir danke und gesegnete ✠ das und brach es, und
> gab es seinen jugern, sagende: Nemet hin und esset all darvon. *Elevando manu dicit:*
> Das ist mein leichnam, der vor euch dargegeben wirt. *Vertens se minister, accipiens
> calicem coram vulgo dicit:* Desselbigen gleichen do man gessen hatte, nam er den kelch
> in seine heiligen, wirdigen hende, und sagete dir dank, und gesegnete ✠ den und gab in
> seinen jugern, sagende: Dis ist der kelch meines blutes, des neuen und ewigen
> testamentes, ein geheim des glaubens, der vor euch und vor viel vorgossen wirt in
> vorzeihunge der sunde. *Rursus vertens se ad altare dicit:* So oft und dick ir das thut,
> solt ir meiner darbei gedenken etc.
> Zum zehenden. Balt nach der elevation singet man im selben ton flux drauf also: Darumb
> last uns alle bitten, wie uns Jesus Christus, der warhaftig son gotis, hat geleret,
> sagende: Vater unser, der du bist in himmeln etc.; und alles volk singet drauf: Amen.
> Darnach wirt es stille, ein wenig, athem zu holen, unter wilcher zeit der priester der
> communicanten halben das sacrament teilet und singet: Durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit.
> So antwort das gemein volk: Amen. Der priester wider: Der fride des herren sei alzeit
> mit euch. So antwort das volk: Und mit deinem geist. Bald nach disem, auf das ein geheim
> gotis, der tod und auferstehung Christi betrachtet werde, das selbe weiter zu erkleren,
> singet alles volk das gezeugnis Joannis des teufers Christi zu dreimal: O lamb gottes,
> wilchs du weg nimpst die sunde der welt etc.; do zu singt man aus dem evangelio Luce 17:
> Erbarme dich unser, und zum letzten: Gib uns deinen fride. […] Auf solchen glauben gibt
> man dann den leuten das hochwirdigste sacrament unter dem agnus dei, on die bepstische
> heuchlische beicht.

Ninthly: Then is sung the form of the consecration, or of the supper, in the tone of the
Preface, according to these words following: The day before, when Jesus would suffer, he
took the bread into his holy and worthy hands, and lifted up his eyes to heaven unto thee,
God, his almighty Father, gave thanks unto thee, and blessed ✠ it, and brake it, and gave
it to his disciples, saying: Take, and eat ye all thereof. *Lifting it up with his hand he
saith:* This is my body, which is given for you. *The minister turning, and taking the cup,
saith before the people:* In like manner, when they had eaten, he took the cup into his holy
and worthy hands, and gave thanks unto thee, and blessed ✠ it, and gave it to his
disciples, saying: This is the cup of my blood, of the new and everlasting testament, a
mystery of faith, which is shed for you and for many for the forgiveness of sins. *Turning
again to the altar he saith:* As oft and often as ye do this, ye shall remember me thereby,
etc.
Tenthly. Soon after the elevation one singeth straightway in the same tone thus: Therefore
let us all pray, as Jesus Christ, the true Son of God, hath taught us, saying: Our Father,
which art in the heavens, etc.; and all the people sing thereupon: Amen. Thereafter it
becometh still a little while, to take breath, during which time the priest divideth the
sacrament for the sake of the communicants, and singeth: World without end. Then the
common people answer: Amen. The priest again: The peace of the Lord be alway with you.
Then the people answer: And with thy spirit. Soon after this, that a mystery of God, the
death and resurrection of Christ, may be considered, and to explain the same further, all
the people sing the witness of John the Baptist of Christ three times: O Lamb of God, which
takest away the sin of the world, etc.; thereto is sung out of the Gospel, Luke 17: Have
mercy upon us; and at the last: Give us thy peace. […] Upon such faith the people are then
given the most worthy sacrament under the Agnus Dei, without the popish hypocritical
confession.

**Note.**

- **The fraction.** "unter wilcher zeit der priester der communicanten halben das
  sacrament teilet und singet: Durch alle ewigkeit der ewigkeit". This is exactly the
  Roman place and manner: the priest broke the host during the last words of the embolism
  and sang *Per omnia saecula saeculorum* over the broken host before the *Pax*.
  - **The reason is new.** The priest breaks "der communicanten halben", for the sake of
    the communicants, to share the bread. It is not a rite of the Mass for its own sake.
  - **No commixture** is mentioned.
  - Luther had forbidden the fraction in the same year (§3.3).
- **"termung".** This is Müntzer's word for the consecration ("die wort der termung";
  "die consecration ein termung, wilche nicht allein von einem, sonder durch die ganze
  vorsamlete gemein geschicht"). Sehling does not gloss it.
- **The elevation.** The bread is elevated at the bread-word ("Elevando manu"). The cup is
  shown to the people ("coram vulgo").
- **Communion** is given during the *Agnus Dei*, "on die bepstische heuchlische beicht".
  The *Ordnung* adds that after communion "sagt man got dank uber das volk und gesegnet
  den herrn etc.": the postcommunion and the *Benedicamus* (§4.4).

### 4.4 Postcommunion and *Benedicamus* (Advent Mass)

> O herr gott, steh hart bei uns, das wir von unsern greulichen lastern mügen abzichtung
> thun, nach dem wir uns durch den geist Christi, deines sohnes, mit dir unwiderruflich
> vorbunden haben durch dis heilige zeichen seines zarten fleischs und teuren blutes, der
> mit dir lebet und regiret in einigkeit des heiligen geistes etc.
> Last uns gesegnen den herren. Gott sei ewiglich dank.

O Lord God, stand hard by us, that we may make renunciation of our grievous vices,
forasmuch as we have bound ourselves irrevocably unto thee through the spirit of Christ,
thy Son, by this holy sign of his tender flesh and precious blood; who with thee liveth
and reigneth in the unity of the Holy Ghost, etc.
Let us bless the Lord. Thanks be to God for ever.

**Note.**

- **The postcommunion.** It is not a Roman text. The sacrament as a "heiliges zeichen" of
  a covenant bond fits Müntzer's teaching. "Zart fleisch und teures blut" are the
  vocabulary of late-medieval eucharistic devotion (compare Kantz's "zarten fronleichnam
  und rosenfarbes blut", §2.1).
- ***Benedicamus Domino / Deo gratias*** replaces *Ite, missa est*, as in the *Formula
  missae*.
- **The Whitsun postcommunion.** The other Masses have their own postcommunions. The Whitsun
  one ("O herr vorlei uns die gnad des heiligen geists, auf das der thau deiner güte,
  unsern grund des herzens in seiner besprengung fruchtbar mache", Sehling 1, 504) renders
  the Roman Whitsun postcommunion *Sancti Spiritus, Domine, corda nostra mundet infusio: et
  sui roris intima aspersione fecundet*. Calenberg-Göttingen 1542 took it over (§13.4).

---

## 5. Strasbourg, the early German Masses, 1524–1525

Sehling 20, Straßburg, Nr. 3 "Die frühen Agenden", pp. 120–162 (`eko.db` doc 1279). Sehling
prints six forms:

| | Form | Pages |
|---|---|---|
| a | Theobald (Diebold) Schwarz's manuscript agenda, "Anfang der evangelischen meß", 1524 | 120–123 |
| b | *Teutsche Meß und Tauff, wie sye yetzund zu Straßburg gehalten werden*, 1524 | 130–134 |
| c | *Ordenung und inhalt Teutscher Mess*, Augsburg reprint, 1524 | 135 |
| d | *Teutsch Kirchenampt mit lobgsengen*, 1524/25 | 136–141 |
| e | *Ordnung des Herren Nachtmal, so man die mesß nennet*, 1525 | 142–150 |
| f | *Straßburger kirchen ampt*, 1525 | 151–162 |

The Worms Mass (§6) is the Strasbourg Mass of 1524 in use at Worms. Its Canon prayers
(*Te igitur* to *Supplices*) are not in Schwarz's text. A later hand added one of them,
the *Unde et memores*, to the manuscript, along with *mysterium fidei* (noted in §5.1). Döber took his optional intercession from
Strasbourg (§8.11).

Across these six forms the Canon is replaced step by step:

- **a–d:** a single intercession in the place of *Te igitur*, with the Verba and a short
  prayer after the elevation.
- **e (1525):** one long prayer "anstatt des Canon" after a shortened common Preface and
  Sanctus. The Verba are read afterwards as a lesson, and there is no elevation.
- **f:** the same prayer, revised, with no Preface or Sanctus at all.

### 5.1 Schwarz's Mass, 1524 (form a)

> *Ad populum versus dicat:* Lieben brüder und schwestern, bitten Gott, den vatter, durch
> unsern hernn Jesum Christum, das er uns den heiligen geist, den tröster, zuschicke, das
> er mache unser leib zu eim lebendigen, heiligen, wolgefalligen opfer, das do ist der
> vernunfftig gotts dienst, der Gott gefelt. Das beschehe uns allen, Amen.
> *Versus populum adhuc stans dicat:* Der herr sey mit euch.
> *Prefatio*
> Erhebt euwere hertzenn. Sagen danck dem herren, unserem Gott. Es geburt sich furwor und
> ist billich, recht und heilsam, das wir dir alweg an allen orten danck sagen. O herr,
> heiliger, almechtiger vatter, ewiger Gott, der du unser heil durch das holtz des creutzs
> verschafft hast, uff das das leben von solchem keme, von welchem der todt uffgangen ist,
> und uff das der feyndt, so durch das holtz übertrettung uns alle in Adam überwunden hatt,
> wider uß gehorsam, so am holtz geleistet ist, bestritten wurde, durch Christum Jesum,
> unsern hern, durch welches maiestat und herlichkeyt dich die engel und alle hymmelsche
> ritterschafft loben, mit glichem frolocken samenthafft ryemen und preysen, zu welchen du
> auch unsere stymmen annemen wöllest, bitten wir mit undertheniger bekantnuß und sagen:
> *Sanctus*
> Sanctus. Heiliger, heiliger herr, Gott der herscharen, voll synd die hymmel und erden von
> deiner herlichkeyt. Ach, mach uns selig in der höhe.
> *Benedictus*
> Er ist zu loben, der do kumpt im namen des herren. Ach, selige uns in der höhe.

*Turned toward the people let him say:* Dear brethren and sisters, pray God the Father,
through our Lord Jesus Christ, that he send us the Holy Ghost, the Comforter, that he may
make our bodies a living, holy, well-pleasing sacrifice, which is the reasonable service of
God that pleaseth God. May this befall us all, Amen.
*Toward the people, still standing, let him say:* The Lord be with you.
*The Preface*
Lift up your hearts. Let us give thanks unto the Lord our God. It becometh us truly, and is
meet, right and wholesome, that we should alway and in all places give thanks unto thee, O
Lord, holy, almighty Father, everlasting God, who hast wrought our salvation through the
wood of the cross, that life might come from thence whence death arose, and that the enemy
who by the transgression of the tree had overcome us all in Adam might be vanquished again
by the obedience rendered on the tree; through Christ Jesus our Lord, through whose majesty
and glory the angels and all the heavenly host praise thee, and together with like
exultation extol and magnify thee; with whom we pray thee to receive our voices also, with
humble confession, saying:
*Sanctus*
Sanctus. Holy, holy Lord, God of hosts, the heavens and earth are full of thy glory. Ah,
save us in the highest.
*Benedictus*
Praised be he that cometh in the name of the Lord. Ah, save us in the highest.

**Note.**

- **The bidding replaces the *Orate fratres*.** The Roman request that "my sacrifice and
  yours may be acceptable" becomes a prayer for the Spirit, who makes "our bodies" the
  sacrifice (Rom 12:1). The only sacrifice named is that of the worshippers.
- **The Preface** is the Preface of the Cross (Passiontide): *qui salutem humani generis in
  ligno Crucis constituisti: ut unde mors oriebatur, inde vita resurgeret: et qui in ligno
  vincebat, in ligno quoque vinceretur: per Christum Dominum nostrum. Per quem maiestatem
  tuam laudant Angeli …*. Form b prints further proper Prefaces (Christmas, Epiphany,
  Easter, Ascension, Whitsun) "for when thou hast read … Ewiger Gott".
- **The Sanctus.** "Ach, mach uns selig" and "Ach, selige uns" translate *Hosanna*.

> *Laventur manus. Tacite. Deinde stans per modum orantis: Levatis, si placet, manibus:*
> Almechtiger, barmhertziger vatter, die weil dein sun, unser herr Jesus, zugesagt hatt,
> was wir bitten in synem namen, das werdest du uns geweren, und zu dem, dyweil dein geist
> auch bevolhen hatt, das wir fur unser oberkeyt bitten sollen, bitten wir von hertzen, du
> wollest die gemütter des keysers, der fursten und herren, vorab unser hern und öberen
> eins ersamen raths, zu erkantnuß dyner guthe und des evangelions bewegen, auch das du
> deynem sun durch den heiligen geist underthenig machest alle volcker, uff das sie selbs
> willigk seyn verheissung erkennen, annemen und behutten. Und vor ab disser gemeyn
> verleyhe, das sie zuneme in erkantnuß des evangelions und seynes sussen jochs und
> gemachsamen burden. Dyweyl nun, almechtiger, ewiger Gott, geliebter und barmhertziger
> vatter,
> Welcher am nechsten tag vor seym lyden das brott nam in sein heilige hend und dir, Gott,
> seynem hymmelschen vatter, dancksaget, segnets, brachs und gabs seynen jungern und
> sprach: Nemmet hien und essend. Das ist meyn lyb, der fur euch geben wurt.
> *Ad calicem:* Des glichen noch dem nachtmal nam er den kelch in sein heilige hende und
> danckt und sprach: Nement hien und trinckent alle daruß. Das ist der kelch meins bluts,
> des neuwen und ewigen testaments, das fur euch und vil vergossen wurt zu vergebung der
> sunden.
> *Elevatio calicis:* Als offt ir das thunt, so thut es in meyner gedechtnuß.

*Let the hands be washed. Silently. Then standing in the manner of one praying: With hands
lifted up, if it please:*
Almighty, merciful Father, forasmuch as thy Son, our Lord Jesus, hath promised that
whatsoever we ask in his name thou wilt grant us; and moreover, forasmuch as thy Spirit
also hath commanded that we should pray for our rulers: we pray thee from our hearts that
thou wouldest move the minds of the emperor, the princes and lords, and first of all of
our lords and superiors of the honourable Council, to the knowledge of thy goodness and of
the Gospel; and also that thou wouldest make all peoples subject to thy Son through the
Holy Ghost, that they themselves may willingly know, receive and keep his promise. And
above all grant unto this congregation that it may increase in the knowledge of the Gospel
and of his sweet yoke and easy burden. Forasmuch now, almighty, everlasting God, beloved and
merciful Father, —
