> gedacht werden. Des zum gewisen warzeichen gib ich euch mein plut zu trinken.
> Wer nun also von disem prot isset, und von disem kelch trinket, das ist: Wer diesen
> worten, die er horet, und disen zaichen, die er empfahet, festiglich glaubet, der bleibt
> in Christo und Christus in im und lebet also ewiglich. Darbei söllen wir an seines tods
> gedenken und ime darumb danksagen, ain jedlicher sein creuz auf sich nemen und ime
> nachfolgen und zuvor ainer den andern lieb haben, wie er auch uns geliebt hat, dann wir
> vil sein ain prot und ain leib, die wir alle aines prots taihaftig sein und aus ainem
> kelch trinken. Das verleihe uns Got allen, das wirs wirdiglich empfahen. Amen.

*After this the priest turneth to the people and saith the exhortation, as hereafter
followeth.*
My dearly beloved in God! Forasmuch as we will now consider and keep the supper of our Lord
Jesus Christ, wherein his flesh and blood are given us for meat and for drink, not of the
body but of the soul, we ought rightly with great diligence every one to examine himself,
as Paul saith, and so to eat of this bread and drink of this cup. For none but a hungry
soul, which knoweth its sin, feareth God's wrath and death, and is hungry and thirsty after
righteousness, ought to receive this holy sacrament. But when we examine ourselves, we find
nothing in us but sin and death, and could in no wise help ourselves out thereof. Therefore
hath our dear Lord Jesus Christ had mercy upon us, and for our sakes is become man, that he
might fulfil the law for us and suffer that which we had deserved with our sins. And that we
might steadfastly believe this and joyfully rely thereupon, he took after the supper the
bread, gave thanks, brake it, and said: Take and eat! This is my body, which is given for
you; as if he would say: That I am become man, and all that I do and suffer, that is all
your own, done for you and for your good. For a sure token thereof I give you my body for
meat.
Likewise also the cup, and said: Take and drink ye all of this! This is the cup of the new
testament with my blood, which is shed for you and for many for the forgiveness of sins. As
oft as ye do this, ye shall remember me thereby! As if he would say: Forasmuch as I have
taken your cause upon me and laden your sins upon myself, I will offer myself unto death for
sin, shed my blood, obtain grace and forgiveness of sins, and so set up a new testament,
wherein sin shall be remembered no more for ever. For a sure token thereof I give you my
blood to drink.
Whosoever now eateth thus of this bread and drinketh of this cup, that is, whosoever firmly
believeth these words which he heareth and these signs which he receiveth, he dwelleth in
Christ and Christ in him, and so liveth for ever. Hereby shall we remember his death and
give him thanks therefor, every one take up his cross and follow him, and above all love one
another, as he also hath loved us; for we being many are one bread and one body, who are all
partakers of one bread and drink of one cup. God grant us all this, that we may receive it
worthily. Amen.

**Note.** Sehling does not reprint the text here. He points to the order of the parish
churches of 1524 (Sehling 11, I.5, p. 48), whose base text is **Osiander's** exhortation
from the provosts' *Grundt und ursach* (1524).

The text above is that base text with **Döber's readings (Sehling's siglum D)** taken from
his apparatus:

- *Mein allerliebsten*;
- *kunden auch uns selbs in keinen weg*;
- *das er für uns*;
- *zur verzeihung*;
- *ewig nicht soll gedacht*;
- *zum gewisen warzeichen*;
- *in Christo* without *dem Herren*;
- *lieb haben*;
- the closing sentence *Das verleihe uns Got allen …*, which p. 54 confirms.

One point is uncertain. For "Des zu [ainem] gewissen warzaichen" (the bread) the apparatus
is garbled; it seems to say D lacks *ainem*.

In Nürnberg this exhortation stood where the Latin Verba had been read silently. Once the
Verba were sung aloud in German, Sehling notes, the exhortation "doubled" them.

### 8.8 *Domine Iesu Christe, Fili Dei vivi*, said by the people

> *Nach diser exhortation wendt sich der priester zum altar und spricht zum volk: Sprecht
> mir nach:*
> O Herr Jesu Christe, du ewigs wort des Vaters, du Heiland der welt, du warer, lebendiger
> Got und mensch, erlöse uns durch deinen heiligen fronleichnam und rosenfarbes blut von
> allen sünden! Hilf, das wir erfüllen deine gebot zu allen zeiten und von dir nicht
> gescheiden werden in ewigkeit! Amen.

*After this exhortation the priest turneth to the altar and saith to the people: Say after
me:*
O Lord Jesus Christ, thou eternal Word of the Father, thou Saviour of the world, thou true,
living God and man, deliver us by thy holy body and rose-red blood from all sins! Help that
we may fulfil thy commandments at all times, and be not separated from thee for ever! Amen.

**Note.** This is *Domine Iesu Christe, Fili Dei vivi, qui ex voluntate Patris, cooperante
Spiritu Sancto, per mortem tuam mundum vivificasti: libera me per hoc sacrosanctum Corpus et
Sanguinem tuum ab omnibus iniquitatibus meis, et universis malis: et fac me tuis semper
inhaerere mandatis, et a te numquam separari permittas*. Sehling calls it "leicht
umgestaltet", as in Volprecht's Mass.

- **Opening.** The Trinitarian opening is replaced by titles of Christ.
- **Number and speaker.** *Me* becomes *uns*.
- **Words for body and blood.** *Fronleichnam* is the old word for the Lord's body (as in
  *Fronleichnamsfest*, Corpus Christi). *Rosenfarbes blut* is a late-medieval devotional
  epithet.
- **Who says it.** The priest's private preparation before his communion is now said by the
  people after him.

### 8.9 Communion

> *Nun communicirt er das volk von ersten, ob er will, und so er in den leib Christi
> darreicht, spricht er zu in:*
> Der leib Christi bewar dein seel zum ewigen leben!
> *Desgleichen spricht er auch, wenn ers nimbt. Und darnach nimbt er den kelch und
> spricht:*
> Das blut unsers lieben Herren Jesu Christi, das für mich und für euch vergossen ist in
> vergebung der sünde, bewar mein seel zum ewigen leben!
> *Und wenn er sumirt hat, bind er den kelch ein.*

*Now he communicateth the people first, if he will; and when he reacheth them the body of
Christ, he saith unto them:*
The body of Christ preserve thy soul unto everlasting life!
*The same he saith also when he taketh it himself. And thereafter he taketh the cup and
saith:*
The blood of our dear Lord Jesus Christ, which is shed for me and for you unto the
forgiveness of sins, preserve my soul unto everlasting life!
*And when he hath consumed, he bindeth up the cup.*

**Note.** These are *Corpus Domini nostri Iesu Christi custodiat animam tuam in vitam
aeternam* and the priest's *Sanguis Domini nostri Iesu Christi custodiat animam meam …*,
with "das für mich und für euch vergossen ist in vergebung der sünde" added.

"Bind er den kelch ein" means he wraps the chalice in its bag.

### 8.10 *Nunc dimittis*, postcommunion, dismissal and blessing

> *Nach demselben spricht er zum volk: Sprecht mir nach:*
> O Herr, nun laß im frid deinen diener nach deinem wort; dann unsere augen haben gesehen
> deinen heiland, welchen du bereitet hast vor dem angesicht aller völker, ein licht, zu
> erleuchten die heiden und zu einer glori deines volks Israhel. Dir sei lob, ehr und dank,
> o du heilige, gebenedeite, herliche Dreifeldigkeit, Got Vater, Got Sun und Heiliger
> Geist! Amen.
> *Nach disem kert sich der priester zu dem volk und spricht also:*
> Der Herr sei mit euch!
> *Das volk:* Und mit deinem geist!
> *Darnach die collecten spricht zum volk:* Last uns bitten:
> O Herr, allmechtiger Got, verleih uns in unser gemüt und herzen, das wir durch den
> zeitlichen tod deines Suns, welche dise wirdige geheimnus bedeuten, das wir getrauen, das
> du uns geben hast das ewig leben durch den Christum, unsern Herrn. Amen.
> *Zu dem letzten kert er sich umb zum volk und spricht:*
> Der Herr sei mit euch!
> *Das volk:* Und mit deinem geist!
> *Der priester:* Saget dank dem Herren!
> *Das volk:* Got sei lob und dank!
> *Der priester:* Neigt eur herz zu Got, so will ich euch den segen geben! *Und spricht:*
> Der Herr gebenedei dich und behüt dich und erleucht sein angesicht über dich und erbarm
> sich dein und wend sein angesicht zu dir und geb dir den ewigen frid im namen des Vaters
> und Suns und des Heiligen Geists! Amen. Gehet hin im frid des Herren!
> *Nun gehet der priester hinweg vom altar.*
> So habt ir das end diser evangelische meß. Got wöl uns verleihen, was uns nütz sei zu
> seel und leib! Amen.

*After the same he saith to the people: Say after me:*
O Lord, now let thy servant depart in peace according to thy word; for our eyes have seen
thy salvation, which thou hast prepared before the face of all peoples, a light to lighten
the Gentiles and to be the glory of thy people Israel. To thee be praise, honour and thanks,
O thou holy, blessed, glorious Trinity, God the Father, God the Son and Holy Ghost! Amen.
*After this the priest turneth to the people and saith thus:*
The Lord be with you!
*The people:* And with thy spirit!
*Thereafter he saith the collect to the people:* Let us pray:
O Lord, almighty God, grant unto us in our mind and heart that, through the temporal death
of thy Son, which these worthy mysteries signify, we may trust that thou hast given us
everlasting life; through the Christ our Lord. Amen.
*Last of all he turneth about to the people and saith:*
The Lord be with you!
*The people:* And with thy spirit!
*The priest:* Give thanks unto the Lord!
*The people:* God be praise and thanks!
*The priest:* Incline your hearts unto God, and I will give you the blessing! *And he
saith:*
The Lord bless thee and keep thee, and lighten his countenance upon thee, and have mercy
upon thee, and turn his countenance unto thee, and give thee everlasting peace; in the name
of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost! Amen. Go ye in the peace of the Lord!
*Now the priest goeth away from the altar.*
So have ye the end of this evangelical Mass. God grant us that which is profitable for soul
and body! Amen.

**Note.**

- **The collect.** It follows the Roman postcommunion of Wednesday in Holy Week: *Largire
  sensibus nostris, omnipotens Deus: ut per temporalem Filii tui mortem, quam mysteria
  veneranda testantur, vitam te nobis dedisse perpetuam confidamus*. Sehling calls it
  "anscheinend neu geschaffen"; the Roman source is identified here for the first time. Note that *testantur* ("bear
  witness to") is rendered *bedeuten*.
- **The dismissal.** "Saget dank dem Herren … Got sei lob und dank" answers to *Benedicamus
  Domino / Deo gratias*. Sehling reads "Gehet hin im frid des Herren" as a paraphrase of
  *Ite, missa est*.

### 8.11 Optional intercession in the place of *Te igitur*

> *Es volgt ein gemein gebet, an einem feiertag zu sprechen, wenn vil volks da ist, nach
> der prefation oder für das Sanctus, wie man wil.*
> Almechtiger, ewiger Got und barmherziger Vater. Dieweil dein Sun, unser Herr Jesus
> Christus, zugesagt hat, was wir bitten in seinem namen, das werdestu uns gewehren, und zu
> dem, dieweil dein Geist auch bevohlen hat, das wir für unser oberigkeit bitten sollen,
> bitten wir von herzen, du wöllest die gemüter des kaisers, der fürsten und herrn, vorab
> unser herren und öbern ains ersamen rats zu erkantnus deiner güte und des evangelions
> bewegen, auch das du deinem Sun durch den Hailigen Gaist undertenig machest alle völker,
> auf das si selbs willig sein verheißung erkennen, annemen und behüten, und vorab diser
> gemein verleihe, das si zuneme in erkantnus des evangelions und seines süßen jochs und
> gemachsamen bürden. Diweil nu, almechtiger, ewiger Got, geliebter und barmherziger Vater,
> dein ainiger Sun, unser Herr Jesus, in die welt kumen ist zu ainem arzt der kranken und
> nit der gesunden und aber unser blindheit die gegenwertige schand der sunden durch sich
> selbs nit sehen noch für krankhait erkennen mag, dann wir leider vergift sind und in unser
> irrung und übertretung uns selbs gefallen, die gebot Gottes hassen, die laster lieben, so
> biten wir, du wöllest durch Got den Heiligen Geist dein gesatz in unser herzen schreiben
> und die verborgen sünd in uns lebendig machen und also uns verleihen, das wir brüfen und
> empfinden mögen, wie unmüglich es uns ist, guts zu tun, damit wir ein hunger und durst
> gewinnen zu der genaden und gerechtigkait, so vor dir allein gilt, welche du der welt
> geben hast durch Christum Jesum unsern Herren.
> *Nach disem volgt das Qui pridie. Gott sei lob!*

*There followeth a common prayer, to be said on a feast day when much people is present,
after the Preface or before the Sanctus, as one will.*
Almighty, everlasting God and merciful Father: forasmuch as thy Son our Lord Jesus Christ
hath promised that whatsoever we ask in his name, thou wilt grant it us; and moreover,
forasmuch as thy Spirit also hath commanded that we should pray for our magistrates: we
beseech thee from our hearts that thou wouldest move the minds of the emperor, the princes
and lords, and above all of our lords and superiors of an honourable council, unto the
knowledge of thy goodness and of the Gospel; and also that thou wouldest make all peoples
subject unto thy Son through the Holy Ghost, that they themselves may willingly acknowledge,
receive and keep his promise; and above all grant unto this congregation that it may
increase in the knowledge of the Gospel, and of his sweet yoke and easy burden. Forasmuch
now, almighty, everlasting God, beloved and merciful Father, as thine only Son our Lord
Jesus is come into the world to be a physician of the sick and not of the whole, and yet our
blindness cannot of itself see the present shame of sin nor know it for a sickness, for we
are, alas, poisoned, and please ourselves in our error and transgression, hate the
commandments of God and love vices: we pray thee that thou wouldest, through God the Holy
Ghost, write thy law in our hearts and make the hidden sin alive in us, and so grant us that
we may prove and perceive how impossible it is for us to do good; that we may gain a hunger
and thirst after the grace and righteousness which alone availeth before thee, which thou
hast given to the world through Christ Jesus our Lord.
*After this followeth the Qui pridie. God be praised!*

**Note.** Sehling traces the prayer to the Strasbourg order of 1524. Its rubric puts it
exactly where *Te igitur* stood: after the Preface, before the Sanctus in Döber's order, and
leading into *Qui pridie*. It intercedes for the emperor, princes and council, for all
peoples, and for "this congregation". These are the intentions of *Te igitur* and the
*Memento*, turned into an evangelical prayer for faith and for the knowledge of sin.

---

## 9. Bremen, *Eyne evangelysce Misse*, 1525 (Kantz in Low German)

*Eyne evangelysce Misse myt etlijke schone gebede …* Bremen 1525. Sehling 7, III, Nr. 1,
pp. 403–407 (`eko.db` doc 2216).

The Mass itself is Kantz's order of 1522 (§2) in Low German. Before it stand three
devotional prayers for the communicant. The second is a "betrachtinge by der Misse", a
meditation during Mass. Its opening confession of the Real Presence is the nearest thing in
the order to a Canon.

The foreword warns that the prayers are no rule: they are meant to show "what faith and
desire are needed to receive the testament of Christ in a Christian manner".

### 9.1 The meditation during Mass

> *Eyn betrachtinge by der Misse*
> Almechtige, ewige Got, mijn here Jesu Christe, Ick gelove, dat in dussem brode
> jegenwordich sy dijn hillige ware licham unde in dussem wijne dijn durbar blot angenamen
> hefst, um Adam unde al syne nakomelinge to verlosen van den ewigen dode. Unde heft uns
> gelaten in dussem hochwerdigen Sacramente eyn seker, wisse teken dijner waraftiger
> beloftenisse, de sunden to vergeven, dat dijne worde betugen, de in der Missen gesproken
> werden:
> Nemet hen unde etet. Dat is mijn licham, dat voer iw gegeven wert.
> Nemet hen unde drincket dar alle af. Dit is mijn bloet, dat vor iw vergaten wert to
> vergevinge der sunden.
> Up sulken trosteliken toseggen begere ick wt mynen gantsen herten, in rechter tovorsicht
> unde guden vortruwen, dat du my nicht latest leddich unde hongerich ungespiset van hier
> gan, sunder spisen wilst unde laven myt dijn levendige woert, Welker uns vercundiget
> vergevinge unser sunden dorch dyn licham unde bloet, Uppe dat ick in warer liften jegen
> dy unde broderlike trwe jegen mynen nechsten bevestiget mach werden.

*A meditation at the Mass*
Almighty, everlasting God, my Lord Jesu Christ, I believe that in this bread is present thy
holy true body, and in this wine thy precious blood, which thou hast taken upon thee to
redeem Adam and all his posterity from everlasting death. And thou hast left us in this
most worthy Sacrament a sure and certain sign of thy true promise to forgive sins, which
thy words witness that are spoken in the Mass:
Take and eat. This is my body, which is given for you.
Take and drink ye all thereof. This is my blood, which is shed for you for the forgiveness
of sins.
Upon such comfortable promise I desire from my whole heart, in right confidence and good
trust, that thou let me not go hence empty and hungry, unfed, but wilt feed and refresh me
with thy living word, which proclaimeth unto us the forgiveness of our sins through thy
body and blood; that I may be established in true love toward thee and brotherly
faithfulness toward my neighbour.

**Note.**

- **Who is addressed.** The prayer is addressed to Christ as "Almechtige, ewige Got" and
  confesses the Real Presence "in dussem brode … in dussem wijne".
- **The Verba** are quoted from "the words spoken in the Mass": the priest's words are
  now heard by the congregation.
- **The Roman background.** It is the priest's private preparation for communion, of the
  kind in the late-medieval *Praeparatio ad missam*, given to the laity.

### 9.2 The Mass: preparation

The preparation follows Kantz (§2.1–2.2) closely:

- the exhortation "Alderlevesten in Christo, Uppe dat gy myt vroliken unde begerliken
  herten entfangen mogen den kosteliken schat unde dat testamente des vlesches unde blodes
  Christi Jesu …";
- the absolution;
- "Bicht des presters", the recast *Orate fratres*: "biddet oeck Got trwelick vor my, dat
  he my mijne sunden vergevende, bequeme make iw syn tekene to delen" ("that he … make me
  meet to distribute his signs to you");
- Ps 124:8;
- the *Veni Sancte Spiritus* antiphon, said "hemelick" by all;
- Kantz's collect, "O Here, almechtige Got, help, dat by uns sy unde wane dyn hillige
  geist …".

### 9.3 Preface, Sanctus, *Quam oblationem* and *Qui pridie*

> *Prefacio ofte vorrede der Missen*
> Vorhevet iw herten to Gade unde latet uns danckseggen Gade, unsen heren. Went et is
> billick, recht unde heilsam, dat wy in allen orden unde to aller tyt dy here, hillige
> vader, almechtige, ewige Got, danckseggen dorck Christum, unsen leven heren, Dorch welken
> alle engelen unde hemmelsche crachte anbeden, eren unde laven dyne hillige maiesteit. Myt
> den sulvigen bidden wy, wilt to laten unde annemen unse stemme, dat wy dy oeck laven unde
> spreken sunder ende:
> Sanctus: Dat is: Hillige, Hillige, Hillige, here Got der herscaren. Vul synt hemmel unde
> erde dijner herlicheit. Osanna in den hogesten. Gebenediet sy, de dar kumpt in den name
> des heren. Gelucke unde heil in den hogesten.
> *Hier hevet sick erst de Evangelische Misse an*
> *Eyn gebeth vor an to Gade*
> O Alder guedigeste vader, barmhertige, ewige Got, giff uns, dat wy vast geloven, dat dit
> brot unde wijn wert dat waraftige lijf unde blot dijnes alder levesten sonnes, unses heren
> Jesu Christi, Welcker an deme dage vor synem lijdent nam dat brot in sijne hilligen hande,
> sach up to dem hemmel to dy, synem almechtigen vader, sede danck unde brack dat brot unde
> gaf dat synen jungeren, sprekende:
> Nemet hen unde etet, dat is mijn licham, dat vor iuw gegeven wert.
> Des gelijken, na dem aventmal, nam he den kellick in syne hillige hande, danckede synen
> vader unde gaf synen jungeren unde sprak:
> Nemet hen unde drincket alle daruth, dat is de kellick des nijgen unde ewigen
> testamentes in mijnen blode, dat vor iuw unde vor vele vorgaten wert to vorgevinge der
> sunden. So vaken alse gy dat doet, so doet dat in mijne gedechtenisse.

*The Preface, or foreword of the Mass*
Lift up your hearts to God, and let us give thanks unto God our Lord. For it is meet, right
and wholesome that we should in all places and at all times give thanks unto thee, O Lord,
holy Father, almighty, everlasting God, through Christ our dear Lord, through whom all
angels and heavenly powers worship, honour and praise thy holy majesty. With the same we
beseech thee, vouchsafe to admit and receive our voice, that we also may praise thee and
say without end:
Sanctus: That is: Holy, holy, holy, Lord God of hosts. Full are heaven and earth of thy
glory. Hosanna in the highest. Blessed be he that cometh in the name of the Lord. Luck and
salvation in the highest.
*Here beginneth first the Evangelical Mass*
*A prayer to God beforehand*
O most gracious Father, merciful, everlasting God, grant us that we may firmly believe that
this bread and wine becometh the true body and blood of thy most dear Son, our Lord Jesus
Christ; who on the day before his passion took the bread into his holy hands, looked up to
heaven unto thee, his almighty Father, gave thanks, and brake the bread, and gave it to his
disciples, saying:
Take and eat, this is my body, which is given for you.
Likewise, after the supper, he took the cup into his holy hands, thanked his Father, and
gave it to his disciples, and spake:
Take and drink ye all thereout, this is the cup of the new and everlasting testament in my
blood, which is shed for you and for many for the forgiveness of sins. As often as ye do
this, do it in my remembrance.

**Note.**

- **The recast *Quam oblationem*.** Sehling's note: "Dieses Gebet entspricht weitgehend
  dem Gebet *Quam oblationem* des Missale Romanum". Kantz's petition "hilf, das dises brot
  und der wein uns werde und sei …" (*ut nobis … fiat*) has become "giff uns, dat wy vast
  geloven, dat dit brot unde wijn wert …". The prayer no longer asks that the bread
  become the body. It asks that we believe that it does.
- **The Verba.** Kantz's two *benedixit* ("segnet") are dropped.

### 9.4 Our Father, *Agnus Dei*, *Fili Dei vivi*, communion, *Nunc dimittis*

> Lat uns bidden: Unse vader, de du bist in den hemmel. Gehilget werde dijn name. Tokamen
> sy uns dijn ryke. Dijn wille de gesche alse in den hemmel, so oeck up der erden. Unse
> dagelikes brot gif uns huden. Unde vorgif uns unse schult, alse wy vorgeven unse
> schuldeneren. Unde vore uns nicht in vorsokinge, Sunder vorlose uns van allen quaden.
> Dorch unsen heren, Jesum Christum, dijnen sone, de myt dy unde dem hilligen geiste, eyn
> war Got, levet unde regneret in ewicheit.
> *Agnus Dei*
> O du lam Gades, de du dregest de sunde der welt, vorbarme dy unser unde gif uns dijnen
> vrede.
> *Eyn gebet vor der entfanginge des hochwerdigen Sacramentes*
> O Here Jesu Christe, du ewige wort dijnes vaders, Du heilant der werlt, levendig Got,
> vorlose uns dorch dijne grondelose barmherticheit van allen sunden. Help, dat wy
> vorvullen dijne gebade to aller tijdt unde van dy nicht gescheden werden in ewicheit.
> *Hier na scal de priester dat hochwerdige Sacrament geven den, de dat myt eynen
> hungerigen, sundigen herten begerent, also sprekende:*
> Wes getrostet. Wente siet, dit is dat licham unses heren Jesu Christi, dat vor iw geleden
> heft den bitteren doet. Nemet hen unde etet, uppe dattet iw spise unde beware in dat
> ewige levent.
> *Alse se alle berichtet synt myt den licham Christi, so scal de prester nemen den kellick
> myt den durbar blode Christi unde spreken also:*
> Nu wil wy oeck drincken den kelick des heils unde anropen den namen des heren. Siet, gy
> bedrofde, sundige herten, unde weset getrostet. Wente dit is de durbar, kostelike scat
> des blodes uns heren Jesu Christi, darmede gij gekoft sint. Nemet dit unde delet under
> malkanderen to afwasschinge iwer sunden.
> *Went dit alles geschen is, so scal de prester myt der gantzer Christen gemene dancken
> unde laven den heren myt dussen lavesanck seggende:*
> O Here, nu latet in vrede dijnen knecht na dijnen worden, want unse ogen hebben gesien
> dijnen heilant, den du bereidet hefst voer den angesichte alles volckes, Eyn lecht, to
> vorluchtende de heiden unde to eyner glorie dijnes volkes Ysrahel. Dy sy lof unde danck,
> o du hillige, benedide drevoldicheit, Got vader, sonne, hillige geist.

Let us pray: Our Father, which art in heaven. Hallowed be thy name. Thy kingdom come unto
us. Thy will be done, as in heaven, so also on earth. Give us this day our daily bread. And
forgive us our debt, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but
deliver us from all evil. Through our Lord Jesus Christ, thy Son, who with thee and the
Holy Ghost, one true God, liveth and reigneth for ever.
*Agnus Dei*
O thou Lamb of God, that bearest the sin of the world, have mercy upon us and give us thy
peace.
*A prayer before the receiving of the most worthy Sacrament*
O Lord Jesu Christ, thou everlasting Word of thy Father, thou Saviour of the world, living
God, deliver us through thy bottomless mercy from all sins. Help us that we may fulfil thy
commandments at all times, and never be parted from thee for ever.
*Hereafter shall the priest give the most worthy Sacrament to them that desire it with a
hungry, sinful heart, saying thus:*
Be comforted. For behold, this is the body of our Lord Jesus Christ, which suffered for you
the bitter death. Take and eat, that it may feed you and preserve you unto everlasting life.
*When they have all been communicated with the body of Christ, the priest shall take the
cup with the precious blood of Christ, and say thus:*
Now will we also drink the cup of salvation and call upon the name of the Lord. Behold, ye
troubled, sinful hearts, and be comforted. For this is the precious, costly treasure of the
blood of our Lord Jesus Christ, wherewith ye are bought. Take this and divide it among
yourselves to the washing away of your sins.
*When all this is done, the priest shall with the whole Christian congregation thank and
praise the Lord with this song of praise, saying:*
O Lord, now let thy servant depart in peace according to thy words, for our eyes have seen
thy salvation, which thou hast prepared before the face of all people, a light to lighten
the Gentiles and for a glory of thy people Israel. To thee be praise and thanks, O thou
holy, blessed Trinity, God the Father, Son, Holy Ghost.

**Note.**

- **As in Kantz (§2.5–2.7):**
  - the conclusion of the embolism after the Our Father;
  - one *Agnus Dei*;
  - an abridged *Fili Dei vivi*;
  - the *custodiat* ("spise unde beware in dat ewige levent");
  - *Calicem salutaris*;
  - the *Nunc dimittis* with a Trinitarian doxology.
- **What changes from Kantz:**
  - ***Fili Dei vivi.*** Kantz's "erlös uns durch disen deinen heiligen fronleichnam und
    rosenvarbes blut" (*per hoc sacrosanctum Corpus et Sanguinem tuum*) becomes "dorch
    dijne grondelose barmherticheit". Bremen removes the one phrase that ascribed the
    deliverance to the consecrated elements.
  - **The communicants** are "de dat myt eynen hungerigen, sundigen herten begerent".
  - **Omitted.** The showing of the host and the *Pax* are gone. No fraction, as in
    Kantz's first edition.
- **What follows.** A German *Te Deum*.

---

## 10. Lippe, *Deutsche Messe*, [1525–1538] (Müntzer in Low German)

Sehling 22, IX (Lippe, Nachtrag), Nr. 1, pp. 565–569 (`eko.db` doc 1549). It is a
manuscript Mass with notes, for Trinity Sunday.

Its eucharistic part is Müntzer's German Mass (§4) in Low German:

- the *Per omnia saecula* before the Preface;
- the Roman *Qui pridie* with "hoff up syne ogen" and "eyn gheheymnys des ghelavens";
- "Darumme ladt uns alle bydden als uns Jhesus Christus … heyfft gheleret";
- the *Per omnia saecula* after the Our Father;
- *Pax Domini*.

Its propers are those of Volprecht's Trinity Mass (§7.2): Rom 11:33–36, John 15:26–16:4,
the Alleluia *Benedictus es, Domine, Deus patrum nostrorum*, and the *Proficiat*
postcommunion.

Müntzer's own German Mass has no Trinity Mass. The Erfurt *Kirchenamt* of 1525, which
reprinted Müntzer's Masses, added one (Sehling 6, III, p. 812 n. 26, to Calenberg-Göttingen 1542,
§13). An Erfurt print of this kind is the likely source of the Lippe text.

> *Dat offertorium*
> *Vor dat offertorium synget me eyn geystlick leidt, dat ys eyn psalme.*
> *Prefatio*
> Durch alle ewicheit der ewicheit amen.
> De here sy myt iw.
> Und myt dynem geiste.
> Unse herten yn de hoge.
> Hebben wy tho dem heren.
> Wy seggen danck dem heren, unserem Gade.
> Edt ys byllyck unde recht.
> Warlyck, edt is byllick unde recht, dartho ock yst seer heilsam, dat wy dy altydt und yn
> allen enden danck seggen. Here, heillyge vader, almechtige, ewyge Godt, welcker du myt
> dynem eyngebornen soen und dem hilligen geiste eyn enyge Godt bist, eyn enyger here bist.
> Nicht yn eynycheit der personen, sonder yn der enycheit eynes sunderliken wesens. Den dat
> wy durch dyn sulvest apenbarynge von dyner herlicheit gheloven, even dat sulffte holden
> wy underscheiden van dynem sone und dem hilligen geiste, up dat yn der bekentnysse der
> waren und ewyghen godtheit de eghenschop yn den personen und enyccheit [!] yn dem wesen
> und de ghelicheit yn der majestat recht werde anghebetet, welke de engel laven,
> erwerdigen de ertzeengel, dartho Cherubin und Seraphin, de ane unterladt myt
> eyndrechtiger stemmen syngen:
> Heillich, hyllich, hillich bist her Godt der herscharen, hymmel und erde syndt vorfullet
> myt dynem prise. Ach, help uns, de du byst yn den hochsten. Ghesegnet sy, de dar kumpt
> ym namen des heren. Ach gyff gheluck und heyl yn den hochsten.

*The offertory*
*For the offertory a spiritual song is sung, that is, a psalm.*
*The Preface*
World without end. Amen.
The Lord be with you.
And with thy spirit.
Our hearts on high.
We have them unto the Lord.
We give thanks unto the Lord our God.
It is meet and right.
Verily it is meet and right, and moreover very wholesome, that we should always and in all
places give thanks unto thee, Lord, holy Father, almighty, everlasting God, who with thine
only-begotten Son and the Holy Ghost art one only God, art one only Lord; not in the oneness
of a person, but in the oneness of a single being. For that which by thine own revelation
we believe of thy glory, even the same we hold, without difference, of thy Son and of the
Holy Ghost; that in the confession of the true and everlasting Godhead, the propriety in
the persons and the unity in the being and the equality in the majesty may be rightly
worshipped; which the angels praise, the archangels honour, and thereto Cherubim and
Seraphim, who without ceasing sing with one accord of voice:
Holy, holy, holy art thou, Lord God of hosts, heaven and earth are filled with thy praise.
Ah, help us, thou that art in the highest. Blessed be he that cometh in the name of the
Lord. Ah, give luck and salvation in the highest.

**Note.**

- **The offertory.** It keeps its name and its place, as a German hymn.
- **"Durch alle ewicheit der ewicheit amen"** heads the "Prefatio". It is the *Per omnia
  saecula saeculorum. Amen* that ended the silent Secret, as in Müntzer (§4.1).
- **The Preface** is the Trinity Preface, rendered fully:
  - *Qui cum unigenito Filio tuo et Spiritu Sancto unus es Deus, unus es Dominus: non in
    unius singularitate personae, sed in unius Trinitate substantiae …*;
  - *… et in personis proprietas, et in essentia unitas, et in maiestate adoretur
    aequalitas. Quam laudant Angeli atque Archangeli, Cherubim quoque ac Seraphim …*.

  "In der enycheit eynes sunderliken wesens" renders *in unius Trinitate substantiae*
  loosely.
- **The Sanctus.** "Ach, help uns" is *Hosanna*.

> Eynen dach thovoren, do Jhesus wolde lyden, nam he dat brodt yn syne hilligen hende und
> hoff up syne ogen yn den hemmel tho dy, Godt, synem almechtigen vader, und sachte dy
> danck und segende dat und brack dat und gaff dat synen jungeren, seggende: Nemet hen und
> ethet darvan, dat ys myn lichnam, de vor iw dar gegeven wert.
> *Hyr ghyfft he den luden dat sacrament, de syk willen berichten laten. Darna nympt he den
> kelck in de handt und spreckt:*
> Des sulven gheliken, als me ghetten hadde, nam he den kelck yn syne hilligen, werdigen
> hende und sachte dy danck und seghende en und gaff en synen jüngeren, seggende: Nempt hen
> und dryncket all dar uth. Dat ys de kelck mynes blodes, des nyggen und ewygen bundes, eyn
> gheheymnys des ghelavens, de vor iw und vor viel vorgatten wert yn vorgevynge der sunde.
> Also vaken gy dat doen, so schol ghy myner darby gedencken.
> *Hyr ghyfft he den lüden, de syk berichten laten, ok den kelck, under des synget me dat
> leydt: Godt sy gelavet edder: Jhesus Christus unse heylandt edder dat diidesche
> sanctus.*

The day before, when Jesus would suffer, he took the bread into his holy hands, and lifted
up his eyes to heaven unto thee, God, his almighty Father, and said thanks unto thee, and
blessed it, and brake it, and gave it to his disciples, saying: Take and eat thereof, this
is my body, which is given for you.
*Here he giveth the sacrament to the people who will be communicated. Thereafter he taketh
the cup in his hand and saith:*
In like manner, when they had eaten, he took the cup into his holy and worthy hands, and
said thanks unto thee, and blessed it, and gave it to his disciples, saying: Take and drink
ye all thereout. This is the cup of my blood, of the new and everlasting covenant, a
mystery of faith, which is shed for you and for many for the forgiveness of sins. As often
as ye do this, ye shall remember me thereby.
*Here he giveth the people who are communicated the cup also; meanwhile the hymn "Gott sei
gelobet" is sung, or "Jesus Christus unser Heiland", or the German Sanctus.*

**Note.**

- **The Verba** are word for word Müntzer's *Qui pridie* (§4.2), with *mysterium fidei*.
- **The order of communion.** As Luther proposed in the *Deutsche Messe*, the bread is
  given after the bread-word and the cup after the cup-word. So the Our Father, the *Pax*
  and the *Agnus Dei* come *after* communion.

> Darumme ladt uns alle bydden als uns Jhesus Christus, de sone Gades, heyfft gheleret,
> seggende: Wader unse, de du byst yn den hemmelen, ghehylliget werde dyn name, tho kame
> dyn rike. Dyn wylle ghesche up erden alse ym hemmel. Unse dachlike brodt gyff uns hute und
> vorgyff uns unse schuldt, als wy vorgeven unsen schuldeners, und vore uns nicht yn
> vorsokynge, sonder vorlose uns van dem ovel, amen.
> Dat ghesche dorch unsen heren Jhesum Christum, dynen soen, welker myt dy und dem
> hyllighen geiste eyn waer Godt, levet und herschet yn ewicheit, amen.
> Durch alle ewycheit der ewicheit amen.
> De fryde des heren sy altydt myt iwe und myt dynem geiste.
> O laem Gades, de du dregst de sunde der werlt, erbarm dick unser.
> O lam Gades, de du dregst de sunde der werlt, gyff uns dynen frede.
> *Dat commun*
> *Vor dat commun synget me eynen psalme edder geistlick lavesanck. Darna sprickt de
> pharher tho dem volcke:* De here sy myt iw.
> *Antwert:* Unde myt dynem geiste.
> O here Godt, ladt uns tho nütte komen des lyves unde der seelen, de entfangynge des
> hyllighen Sacramentes und de ewighe bekentnysse der hillyghen und der sulffstendigen
> unghedeilden dreyvoldycheit, de du levest und regerest eyn enych Godt dorch alle ewicheit
> thor ewicheit, amen.
> Ladt uns benedyen den hern, Gade sy ewich danck.
> De here benedye dy unde behode dy unde erluchte syn anghesichte aver dy unde erbarme
> syck dyner unde wende syn anghesichte tho dy unde gheve dy den frede, amen.

Therefore let us all pray, as Jesus Christ, the Son of God, hath taught us, saying: Our
Father, which art in the heavens, hallowed be thy name, thy kingdom come. Thy will be done
on earth as in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread, and forgive us our debt, as we
forgive our debtors, and lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil, amen.
This be done through our Lord Jesus Christ, thy Son, who with thee and the Holy Ghost, one
true God, liveth and reigneth for ever, amen.
World without end. Amen.
The peace of the Lord be alway with you, and with thy spirit.
O Lamb of God, that bearest the sin of the world, have mercy upon us.
O Lamb of God, that bearest the sin of the world, give us thy peace.
*The communion*
*For the communion a psalm or spiritual song of praise is sung. Thereafter the pastor saith
to the people:* The Lord be with you.
*Answer:* And with thy spirit.
O Lord God, let the receiving of the holy Sacrament, and the everlasting confession of the
holy and self-subsistent undivided Trinity, profit us for body and soul; who livest and
reignest one only God, world without end, amen.
Let us bless the Lord; thanks be to God for ever.
The Lord bless thee and keep thee, and make his face shine upon thee, and have mercy upon
thee, and turn his face unto thee and give thee peace, amen.

**Note.**

- **The embolism's two endings.** Lippe has both:
  - Kantz's "Dat ghesche dorch unsen heren …", the conclusion *Per eundem Dominum*;
  - Müntzer's "Durch alle ewycheit der ewicheit", the chant ending *Per omnia saecula*.

  There is no fraction rubric.
- ***Pax Domini*** is kept with its response. The response is run on in the manuscript.
- **The postcommunion** is the Roman *Proficiat* of Trinity Sunday, as in Volprecht
  (§7.2).
- **The blessing.** "Ladt uns benedyen den hern, Gade sy ewich danck" is Müntzer's
  *Benedicamus*. The Aaronic blessing follows.

---

## 11. Schleswig-Holstein, a Low German Mass from Kiel, [after 1526]

Sehling 23, A, Nr. 1, pp. 55–57 (`eko.db` doc 1576; introduction in doc 1568, p. 39). The
manuscript, with notes, was bound into a book of the Kiel preacher Rudolf von Nimwegen
(† 1542). Sehling's editor dates it after 1526 because it draws on Döber (1525) and on
Luther's *Deutsche Messe* (1526).

The order also draws on:

- **Kantz** (§2): the *Veni Sancte Spiritus* and its collect.
- **Müntzer** (§4): the Roman *Qui pridie*, with "hoff up syne ogen".
- **Döber** (§8): the *Fili Dei vivi* said by the people after the priest.

It is the only order in the corpus with a German *Domine, non sum dignus*.

### 11.1 Before the altar: absolution by the sexton, *Veni Sancte Spiritus*, collect

> *Minister legit: Myseriator. De coster list: Miseriator.*
> De almechtyge, barmhartyge Got vorgeve uns unße sunde. Unde yck, uth bevele unßes heren
> Jesu Christi, segge jwe, de gy Gades gnaden truwen unde loven synen worden, frigh,
> leddich unde loß alle jwe sunde. Im name des vaders unde des sones unde des hylgen
> geistes.
> *De prester:* Amen.
> *De prester lyst:*
> Kum, hilge geist, erfülle de hartenn dyner gelovygen unde entfenge in en dat vor dyner
> gotliken leve, de du dorch menychfoldycheyt der tungenn de volcker der gantzenn warldet
> vorsamelt heffst in enycheit des waren gelovens.
> *Costos:* Amen.
> *De prester list:*
> O here, almechtige Got, gyff, dat by uns sy unde wane dyne hilge geist, dat he uns
> erluchte unde lere alle warheyt. Sterke unde boschutte yn aller wedderwardicheyt, dorch
> Christum, unsen herren.
> *De coster:* Amen.

*The minister readeth: Misereatur. The sexton readeth: Misereatur.*
The almighty, merciful God forgive us our sins. And I, by the commandment of our Lord Jesus
Christ, say unto you that trust in God's grace and believe his words, free, quit and loosed
of all your sins. In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost.
*The priest:* Amen.
*The priest readeth:*
Come, Holy Ghost, fill the hearts of thy faithful and kindle in them the fire of thy divine
love, who through the diversity of tongues hast gathered the peoples of the whole world in
the unity of the true faith.
*Sexton:* Amen.
*The priest readeth:*
O Lord, almighty God, grant that thy Holy Ghost may be and dwell with us, that he may
enlighten us and teach all truth. Strengthen and defend in all adversity; through Christ
our Lord.
*The sexton:* Amen.

**Note.**

- **The absolution.** "Myseriator" (*Misereatur*) and the absolution are Kantz's (§2.1),
  but read by the sexton. The priest only adds the *Amen*. Sehling's editor points to
  Hadeln 1526 for the same practice.
- **The prayers.** The antiphon and collect are Kantz's (§2.2), said before the priest goes
  to the altar. Then the antiphon is sung again, with notes, and a troped Kyrie follows.

### 11.2 Preface and the bread-word

> De Here sy myt jw. Unße harte yn de hogde. Wy seggen danck dem heren, unßem Gade.
> Warlich, ydt ys werdich unde recht, billich unde ys heilsam, dat wy, Here, almechtyge
> Godt, dy allenthalven danck seggen, unde sunderliken yn desser tydt tho pryßenn, wente
> Cristus, unse paschelam, ys vor uns geoffert. He ys dat wäre lam Gades, welk darwech
> genamen hefft de ßunde der werlde, de dorch synen tytliken dot unsen ewygen dot vorstoret
> hefft. Unde als he upgestan ys, hefft he wedder gebracht dat levent.
> Eyne dach to voren, do Jesus Christus wolde liden, nam he dat brot yn syne hillygen,
> werdigen hende unde hoff up syne ogen in den hemmel tho dy, Got, synem alemechtigen
> vader, unde ßede dy dank unde gesegende ydt unde brack dat unde gaff yt synen jüngeren,
> seggende:
> Nemet hen unde ethet alle darvan. Dyt ys myn licham, de dar vor jw gegeven verth in
> vorgevinge der sunde.
> *Nu hevet dat cor an to syngende: Jhesus Christus, unse hylant. Eyn versch dar van.*

The Lord be with you. Our hearts on high. We give thanks unto the Lord our God.
Verily it is worthy and right, meet and wholesome, that we, O Lord, almighty God, should in
all places give thanks unto thee, and specially at this time praise thee, for Christ our
Passover lamb is offered for us. He is the true Lamb of God, which hath taken away the sin
of the world, who by his temporal death hath destroyed our eternal death; and when he rose
again, he hath brought life again.
The day before, when Jesus Christ would suffer, he took the bread into his holy and worthy
hands, and lifted up his eyes to heaven unto thee, God, his almighty Father, and said thanks
unto thee, and blessed it, and brake it, and gave it to his disciples, saying:
Take and eat ye all thereof. This is my body, which is given for you for the forgiveness of
sins.
*Now the choir beginneth to sing: "Jesus Christus, unser Heiland". One verse thereof.*

**Note.**

- **The Preface** is the Easter Preface: *Te quidem, Domine, omni tempore, sed in hac
  potissimum die gloriosius praedicare, cum Pascha nostrum immolatus est Christus. Ipse
  enim verus est Agnus, qui abstulit peccata mundi. Qui mortem nostram moriendo destruxit,
  et vitam resurgendo reparavit*.
  - The manuscript breaks off before the *Et ideo* and the Sanctus (Sehling: "Der Text
    bricht an dieser Stelle ab").
  - "Ys vor uns geoffert" keeps *immolatus est* (1 Cor 5:7).
- **The bread-word** is Müntzer's *Qui pridie*, with "in vorgevinge der sunde" added to the
  bread.
- **Separate consecration.** Only the bread is consecrated now. The cup is consecrated after
  the communion of the bread (§11.4), as Luther had suggested.

### 11.3 Exhortation, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Domine non sum dignus*

> *Dar na kert sick de prester myt dem licham Cristi thom volchke. Unde de communicanten
> setten syck al up de reghe vor dat altar, dat sacramente tho entfangende. Unde de prester
> deyt eyn vormanynge unde secht, den licham Christi yn der hant haldende:*
> Goden frunde, Cristus, unser heylant, hefft uns in synem latesten affschede nagelaten eyn
> harlich klenade als synen licham unde blot eyner geystlyken artzedye unde spyse, dar wy
> syner scholen by gedencken, dorch welkeren licham unde blot he uns eyn enych testamente
> der salicheyt hefft upgerichtet unde dat sulvyge testamente synes flesches unde blödes myt
> synem bytteren lydende bokrefftyget. Unde ßo vaken wy uns myt sunden folet besweret unde
> uns na dysser spise unde na dyssem drancke hungeret unde dorstet, schole wy yth myt
> eyneme gruntligen unde fasten geloven entfangen. Unde so vaken wy yth tho uns nemen,
> schole wy andechtich ßyn, synes bytteren lydendes eme danck und loff tho seggen vor syne
> woldath unde dat unß desse spyse mege syn helplich unde tröstlich thom ewygem levende.
> Amen.
> Spreket my na myt ynnygem harten:
> O Here Jesu Christi, du sone des levendygen Gades, de du uth vederlykem willen, dorch
> medewarkinge des hilgen gestes, dorch dynen bytteren doth de werlt heffst wedder
> levendich ghemaket dorch den geloven, erlöse uns armen kynder Ade dorch dyssen dynen
> hylgen licham unde unschuldigen bloth van allen unsen ungerechticheyden unde bosheyden
> unde vorlene uns, dat vy alle tydt gehorßam dynen gebaden myt lust unde leve volgen unde
> van dy nummer affgesundert werden. Amen.
> O Here, ick en byn nycht werdich dorch myn vordenst dynes werdigen lichames unde blödes,
> sunder make my werdich dorch dyne gotlike tosage, make my salich dorch dyn heylsame wort.
> Amen.

*Thereafter the priest turneth with the body of Christ to the people. And the communicants
seat themselves all in a row before the altar to receive the sacrament. And the priest
maketh an exhortation and saith, holding the body of Christ in his hand:*
Good friends, Christ our Saviour hath left us at his last departing a glorious jewel, to
wit his body and blood, for a spiritual medicine and food, whereby we shall remember him;
through which body and blood he hath set up for us an only testament of salvation, and
confirmed the same testament of his flesh and blood with his bitter passion. And as often
as we feel ourselves burdened with sins, and hunger and thirst after this food and after
this drink, we shall receive it with a thorough and firm faith. And as often as we take it
unto us, we shall be mindful to give him thanks and praise for his bitter passion, for his
benefit, and that this food may be helpful and comfortable unto us unto everlasting life.
Amen.
Say after me with inward heart:
O Lord Jesu Christ, thou Son of the living God, who by the Father's will, through the
working together of the Holy Ghost, hast by thy bitter death made the world alive again
through faith: deliver us poor children of Adam, by this thy holy body and innocent blood,
from all our iniquities and wickednesses, and grant us that we may at all times follow thy
commandments obediently with desire and love, and never be separated from thee. Amen.
O Lord, I am not worthy by my merit of thy worthy body and blood; but make me worthy by thy
divine promise, make me whole by thy wholesome word. Amen.

**Note.**

- **The priest holds the host** during the exhortation. The exhortation takes the place of
  the Canon's remembrance.
- ***Fili Dei vivi*** is the Strasbourg and Döber German text (§5.1, §8.8), said after
  the priest by the communicants. Three phrases are added:
  - "dorch den geloven" (the world made alive "through faith");
  - "uns armen kynder Ade";
  - "myt lust unde leve".
- ***Domine, non sum dignus.*** The Roman text is *Domine, non sum dignus, ut intres sub
  tectum meum: sed tantum dic verbo, et sanabitur anima mea* (Mt 8:8). It was said by the
  priest, and by the people before communion. The Kiel text changes it:
  - It drops the centurion's roof.
  - It makes the worthiness a question of merit ("dorch myn vordenst").
  - It asks that Christ's "divine promise" make the communicant worthy.
  - "make my salich dorch dyn heylsame wort" keeps *sed tantum dic verbo, et sanabitur*.

### 11.4 Communion of the bread; the cup-word and communion of the cup; blessing

> *Nu vordelet de prester den licham Christi den communicanten, unde dat cor hevet an dat
> ander versus alßo: Dat wy des nummer vorgeten, gaff etc. Unde singet ßo lange, dat de
> licham Christi vordelet is. Dar na swicht dat chor stille. Unde de prester hevet vordan
> an, dat bloth Christi tho consecrerende unde secht:*
> Des sulven gelyken, do men gegeten hade, nam he den kelk yn syne hylligen, werdigen hende
> unde ßede dy dank unde gesegende den unde gaff an synen jüngeren, seggende:
> Nemet hen unde drincket alle daruth. Dyt ys de kelk mynes blödes, des nygen unde ewygen
> testamentes, de vor jw unde vor vele vorgaten wert yn vorgewinghe der sunde. Szo vaken gy
> dith don, ßo dot dat yn myner gedechnisse.
> *Nu gifft de prester den communicanten dat bloth Christi, unde dat chor singet: Jesus
> Christus, unser, edder den lavesanck: Got, de sy gelavet unde gebenediet etc. Unde singet
> ßolange dat bloth Christi vordelet is. Darna ßo ker sick de prester wedder to dem wolke
> [sic] unde secht:*
> De Here sy myt jw.
> *Unde beslut de mysse myt ener collecten. Unde na der collecten secht de prester noch
> eyn mal:* De Here sy myt jw.
> *Dar na list he disse nagescreven segenynge aver dat volck unde secht, Numeri VI.
> capite:*
> De Here segene jw unde bohode jw.
> De Her erluchte syn anghesichte aver jw unde sy jw gnedich.
> De Here hewe syn angesichte uppe jw unde ghewe jw frede, dorch Cristum, unßen heren.
> *Dat Chor antwardet:* Amen.
> *Dar na synget men eynen psalm, dat men de misse dar mede besluth.*

*Now the priest distributeth the body of Christ to the communicants, and the choir beginneth
the second verse thus: "Dass wir des nimmer vergessen, gab etc.", and singeth so long till
the body of Christ is distributed. Thereafter the choir is silent. And the priest
beginneth further to consecrate the blood of Christ, and saith:*
In like manner, when they had eaten, he took the cup into his holy and worthy hands, and
said thanks unto thee, and blessed it, and gave it to his disciples, saying:
Take and drink ye all thereout. This is the cup of my blood, of the new and everlasting
testament, which is shed for you and for many for the forgiveness of sins. As often as ye
do this, do it in my remembrance.
*Now the priest giveth the communicants the blood of Christ, and the choir singeth "Jesus
Christus unser", or the song of praise "Gott sei gelobet und gebenedeiet", etc., and singeth
so long till the blood of Christ is distributed. Thereafter the priest turneth again to the
people and saith:*
The Lord be with you.
*And concludeth the Mass with a collect. And after the collect the priest saith once more:*
The Lord be with you.
*Thereafter he readeth this blessing written hereafter over the people, and saith, Numbers
the sixth chapter:*
The Lord bless you and keep you.
The Lord make his face shine upon you and be gracious unto you.
The Lord lift up his countenance upon you and give you peace, through Christ our Lord.
*The choir answereth:* Amen.
*Thereafter a psalm is sung, wherewith the Mass is concluded.*

**Note.**

- **"dat bloth Christi tho consecrerende".** The rubric calls the cup-word a consecration.
- **The cup-word** is Müntzer's *Simili modo*, but "testamentes" (not "bundes"), and without
  *mysterium fidei*.
- **No Our Father, no *Pax*, no *Agnus Dei*.** The Our Father is said with the Creed and
  the Verba on weekdays without communicants, and at the communion of the sick.

---

## 12. Brandenburg, *Kirchenordnung*, 1540

Church order of the Elector Joachim II, 1540. Sehling 3, Mark Brandenburg Nr. 3, pp. 67–71.

The order is conservative. The Latin chants stay. The offertory chant stays. The Preface
and Sanctus are sung in Latin. The Verba are sung aloud in German. The prayers the priest
said before communion (*Domine Iesu Christe qui dixisti*, *Fili Dei vivi*, *Perceptio*) and
after it (*Corpus tuum*, *Quod ore sumpsimus*) are kept in Latin.

In the Canon's own place, under the Sanctus, the priest says four new German collects. For
the Pfalz-Neuburg version of these, Sehling notes: "Diese Gebete sind an die Stelle der in
der römischen Messe teils vor teils nach der Konsekration gesprochenen Fürbittgebete
gesetzt".

The order's rationale (p. 67) is given before the Mass:

> Es sollen auch verba consecrationis nach der prefation offentlich gesungen oder
> gesprochen werden, wie denn solchs von alters in der kirchen auch ublich gewesen […].
> Daraus abzunemen, mit was grunde solche wort den leien mögen verboten werden. Allein der
> ursach, das man sich vielleicht besorget, sie mochten mit der zeit aus dem gewar und
> innen werden, das inen das sacrament des kelchs sowol zustünde als den priestern und
> villeicht daneben erfaren die ungeschicklichkeit ires canons, darauf sie fast alle
> seligkeit gestelt und doch denselben heimlich gelesen […].

The words of consecration shall also be sung or spoken openly after the Preface, as this
also hath been customary in the Church of old […]. Whence may be gathered with what ground
such words may be forbidden to the laity: only for this cause, that it was perhaps feared
they might in time become aware thereby, and find out, that the sacrament of the cup
belonged to them as well as to the priests; and perhaps besides discover the unfitness of
their Canon, whereon they have set well-nigh all salvation, and yet read the same secretly
[…].

### 12.1 Creed, offertory, Preface, Sanctus

> *Darauf sol der priester singen, credo in unum deum, das patrem, in thumen und stiften sol
> latinisch, aber in den pfarren deudsch: Wir gleuben all an einen gott gesungen werden;
> darauf sol der priester singen: Dominus vobiscum absque oremus; darauf sol der gesang, den
> man hievor pro offertorio gehalten hat, gesungen werden; darauf sol der priester die
> gewönliche prefation singen und folgend das latinisch sanctus alles in latin gesungen
> werden. Unter dem sanctus sol der priester folgende oration deudsch beten.*

*Thereupon the priest shall sing Credo in unum Deum; the Patrem shall be sung in cathedrals
and collegiate churches in Latin, but in the parishes in German, "Wir glauben all an einen
Gott". Thereupon the priest shall sing Dominus vobiscum without Oremus; thereupon the chant
which heretofore was kept for the offertory shall be sung; thereupon the priest shall sing
the customary Preface, and next the Latin Sanctus, all sung in Latin. Under the Sanctus the
priest shall pray the following prayer in German.*

**Note.**

- **The offertory.** "*Dominus vobiscum absque oremus*" keeps the greeting before the
  offertory but drops the *Oremus* that led into the offertory prayers. The offertory chant
  itself survives ("der gesang, den man hievor pro offertorio gehalten hat"). No offertory
  prayers are said.
- **In villages.** On p. 71 the order adds that "in den kleinen flecken … und auf den
  dörfern" the offertory "von der dominica oder festen" may be replaced by a German psalm
  after the sermon: "darauf sol folgen die latinische prefation, das sanctus, die communio
  und ferrer der beschlus".
- **The Preface.** "Die gewönliche prefation" is the Latin Preface of the day. It is not
  printed.

### 12.2 The four collects under the Sanctus (in place of the Canon's intercessions)

> *Fur die oberigkeit.*
> Barmherziger himlischer vater, in welches hende aller menschen gewalt und obrigkeit
> stehet, von dir eingesetzt zur straf der ubeltheter und zum schutz der fromen, in welches
> hende auch stehen alle recht und gesetze aller lender, wir bitten dich, sei gnedig unserm
> herrn kaiser, allen königen und fürsten, sonderlich unserm landesfursten und herrn N. und
> dem rath und regenten dieser stadt und aller ordenlichen obrigkeit, damit sie das
> weltlich schwerd, das du inen befohlen hast, recht füren mügen; umschattige sie mit der
> kraft des allerhöchsten, erleucht und erhalt sie bei deinem göttlichen namen, lass sie
> dein göttlichs wort von herzen lieb haben und darüber fest halten und sie ja dasselbig
> nicht lassen verfolgen, gib inen, lieber herr, weisheit und einen verstand und ein
> fridsam regiment, auf das sie alle ire unterthanen in der warheit und gerechtigkeit, die
> dir gefellig ist, regiren und beschirmen, auch, mein lieber gott, friste inen ir leben
> lang, nach deinem willen und weil das gerichte dein ist und sie an deiner stad sitzen, gib
> inen gnad, also zu regiren, das dein heiliger name durch sie geheiligt und gepreiset
> werde, von nu an bis in ewigkeit, das helf inen der herr Jesu Christ, dein geliebter son,
> amen.
>
> *Ein ander.*
> O almechtiger gütiger gott und vater unsers herrn Jesu Christi, der du uns ernstlich
> befohlen hast, das wir dich bitten sollen um arbeiter in deiner ernten, das ist um
> rechtschaffne prediger deines worts. Wir bitten dein gründlose barmherzigkeit, du wollest
> uns rechtschaffne lerer und diener deines göttlichen worts zuschicken und denselben dein
> heilsams wort in das herz und in den mund geben, das sie deinen befelch treulich
> ausrichten und nichts predigen, das deinem heiligen wort entgegen sei, auf das wir durch
> dein himlisch ewigs wort ermanet, geleret, gespeist, getröst und gesterkt werden, thun,
> was dir gefellig und uns fruchtbarlich ist; gib herr deiner heiligen christlichen kirchen
> deinen geist und göttliche weisheit, das dein wort unter uns laufe und wachse und mit
> aller freidigkeit, wie sichs gebürt, gepredigt und dein heilige christliche gemein
> dadurch gebessert werde, auf das wir mit bestendigem glauben dir dienen und in erkentnis
> deines namens bis an das ende verharren, durch unsern herrn etc.
>
> *Ein ander.*
> Almechtiger gott und ewiger vater, ein könig der ehren und ein herr himels und der erden,
> durch welches geist alle dinge regiret, durch welches fursehung alle dinge geordnet
> werden, du bist ein gott des friedes, von dem allein einigkeit zu uns komt, wir bitten
> dich durch unsern herrn Jesum Christum, du wollest uns unser sunde vergeben und mit
> deinem göttlichen friden und einigkeit begnaden, damit wir in forcht und zittern deinem
> namen dienen und allezeit von uns gepreiset werdest, amen.
>
> Barmherziger gott und himlischer vater, des barmherzigkeit kein ende hat, der du
> langmütig, gnedig und von grosser güte und treu bist und vergibst alle missethat,
> ubertretung und sunde, wir haben leider gesundigt mit unsern vetern, wir haben
> mishandelt und sind gottlos gewesen und dich oftmals erzürnet, dir allein haben wir
> gesundigt und ubel fur dir gethan, aber herr, gedenke nicht an unsere vorige missethat,
> las bald dein barmherzigkeit uber uns gross sein, denn wir sind elend worden, hilf uns
> gott unsers heils um der ehre willen deines namens, erlöse uns und vergib uns unser sunde
> durch deinen son Jesum Christum, amen.

*For the magistrates.*
Merciful heavenly Father, in whose hands standeth all power and authority of men, ordained
of thee for the punishment of evildoers and the protection of the godly, in whose hands also
stand all the rights and laws of all lands: we beseech thee, be gracious unto our lord the
Emperor, all kings and princes, especially unto our prince and lord of the land N., and unto
the council and rulers of this city and all lawful magistrates, that they may rightly bear
the temporal sword which thou hast committed unto them. Overshadow them with the power of
the Highest; enlighten them and keep them in thy divine name; let them love thy divine word
from the heart and hold fast thereto, and in no wise suffer it to be persecuted. Give them,
dear Lord, wisdom and understanding and a peaceable government, that they may rule and
defend all their subjects in the truth and righteousness that is well-pleasing unto thee.
Also, my dear God, prolong their life according to thy will; and forasmuch as the judgement
is thine and they sit in thy stead, give them grace so to rule that thy holy name may be
hallowed and praised through them, from henceforth even for ever. This help them the Lord
Jesus Christ, thy beloved Son. Amen.

*Another.*
O almighty, gracious God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, who hast earnestly commanded us
that we should pray thee for labourers into thine harvest, that is, for right and true
preachers of thy word: we beseech thy bottomless mercy that thou wouldest send us right and
true teachers and ministers of thy divine word, and give thy wholesome word into their heart
and into their mouth, that they may faithfully perform thy commandment and preach nothing
that is contrary to thy holy word; to the end that we, being by thy heavenly, eternal word
admonished, taught, fed, comforted and strengthened, may do that which is well-pleasing
unto thee and profitable unto us. Give, O Lord, unto thy holy Christian Church thy Spirit and
divine wisdom, that thy word may run and grow among us and be preached with all boldness, as
it becometh, and that thy holy Christian congregation may be bettered thereby; that we may
serve thee with steadfast faith and continue in the knowledge of thy name unto the end;
through our Lord, etc.

*Another.*
Almighty God and everlasting Father, a King of glory and a Lord of heaven and of earth, by
whose Spirit all things are governed, by whose providence all things are ordered; thou art a
God of peace, from whom alone unity cometh unto us: we beseech thee through our Lord Jesus
Christ that thou wouldest forgive us our sins and endue us with thy divine peace and unity,
that we may serve thy name in fear and trembling, and that thou mayest be praised of us
evermore. Amen.

Merciful God and heavenly Father, whose mercy hath no end, who art longsuffering, gracious
and of great goodness and faithfulness, and forgivest all iniquity, transgression and sin:
we have, alas, sinned with our fathers, we have done amiss and dealt wickedly and oftentimes
provoked thee to wrath; against thee only have we sinned and done evil in thy sight. But,
Lord, remember not against us our former iniquities; let thy mercy speedily be great upon
us, for we are brought very low. Help us, O God of our salvation, for the glory of thy name;
deliver us and forgive us our sins, through thy Son Jesus Christ. Amen.

**Note.** The texts and translations are the same as in `general_prayers.db` (family L).
Sehling traces the collects for the ministers and for peace to Brandenburg-Nürnberg 1533.
The one for the magistrates is an expanded form of the Nürnberg collect. The penitential
fourth, woven from Ps 106:6, Ps 51:4 and Ps 79:8–9, stands where the Canon's prayers for
forgiveness stood (*Hanc igitur*'s *ab aeterna damnatione nos eripi*, the *Nobis quoque
peccatoribus*).

### 12.3 The Verba, sung, with elevation

> *Darnach verba consecrationis deudsch singen und eleviren wie folgt:*
> Unser herr Jesus in der nacht, do er verraten ward, nam er das brot, danket seinem
> himlischen vater, gebenedeiet es und brachs und gabs seinen jungern und sprach: Nemet hin
> und esset, das ist mein leib, der fur euch dargeben wird, das thut zu meiner gedechtnis.
> *Hic modica inclinatione, leva illud reverenter in altum. Deinde calicem accipe cum
> ambabus manibus, et dic.*
> Desselben gleichen nam er auch den kelch nach dem abendmal, danket und benedeiet in und
> gab den seinen jüngern und sprach: Trinket alle daraus, das ist mein blut des neuen
> testamentes, das vor euch und vor vil vergossen wird zur vergebung der sunden, solchs
> thut, so oft irs trinket, zu meinem gedechtnis.
> *Leua calicem in altum.*
> *Nach der elevation sol man in Thumen und stiften einen latinischen gesang singen, als das
> responsorium tua est potencia etc., in den pfarren aber einen deudschen gesang. Es wolt
> uns gott gnedig sein oder sei lob und dank mit hohem preis.*

*Thereafter the words of consecration shall be sung in German, and the elevation made, as
followeth:*
Our Lord Jesus, in the night in which he was betrayed, took the bread, gave thanks unto his
heavenly Father, blessed it, and brake it, and gave it to his disciples, and said: Take and
eat, this is my body, which is given for you; this do in remembrance of me.
*Here, with a slight inclination, lift it up reverently on high. Then take the cup with both
hands, and say:*
Likewise also he took the cup after the supper, gave thanks and blessed it, and gave it to
his disciples, and said: Drink ye all of it, this is my blood of the new testament, which is
shed for you and for many for the remission of sins; this do, as oft as ye drink it, in
remembrance of me.
*Lift the cup on high.*
*After the elevation, in cathedrals and collegiate churches a Latin chant shall be sung, as
the responsory Tua est potentia, etc.; but in the parishes a German song: "Es wolt uns Gott
genädig sein", or "Sei Lob und Dank mit hohem Preis".*

**Note.**

- **The Verba.** They are the Pauline and Lutheran form. "Danket seinem himlischen vater"
  keeps an echo of *tibi gratias agens*. The print's *gebenedeietes* is divided here as
  "gebenedeiet es".
- **Rubrics.** The Latin rubrics are those of the missal: *elevat … reverenter*, *accipit
  calicem ambabus manibus*.
- **The chant.** The Latin responsory after the elevation takes the place of the
  *Benedictus* sung at the elevation.

### 12.4 Our Father and the Peace

> *Darauf sol, wie hernach folget, das pater noster deudsch gesungen werden.*
> Last uns beten, wie uns der herr Christus Jesus befohlen hat, das wir aus rechter
> zuversicht und vertrauen dorfen sagen: Vater unser, der du bist im himel, geheiliget werde
> dein name, zukome dein reich, dein wil geschehe als im himel auch auf erden, unser teglich
> brod gib uns heute und vergib uns unser schulde als wir vergeben unsern schuldigern und
> für uns nicht in versuchung, sondern erlös uns von dem ubel, denn dein ist das reich und
> die kraft und die herrlichkeit in ewigkeit.
> *Dicat omnis populus, amen.*
> *Vertens se ad populum cantat.*
> Der fried des herren sei mit euch allen.
> *Dicat omnis populus, amen.*

*Thereupon the Pater noster shall be sung in German, as followeth.*
Let us pray, as the Lord Christ Jesus hath commanded us, that out of right confidence and
trust we may be bold to say: Our Father, which art in heaven, hallowed be thy name, thy
kingdom come, thy will be done, as in heaven, so in earth; give us this day our daily bread,
and forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors; and lead us not into temptation, but
deliver us from evil: for thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever.
*Let all the people say, Amen.*
*Turning himself to the people he singeth:*
The peace of the Lord be with you all.
*Let all the people say, Amen.*

**Note.** "Wie uns der herr Christus Jesus befohlen hat, das wir … dorfen sagen" recasts
*Praeceptis salutaribus moniti … audemus dicere*.

There is no embolism. *Pax Domini sit semper vobiscum* is kept, with the people answering
*Amen* where the Roman response is *Et cum spiritu tuo*.

### 12.5 *Agnus Dei* and the priest's three prayers before communion (Latin)

> Agnus dei qui tollis peccata mundi, miserere nobis.
> Agnus dei qui tollis peccata mundi, miserere nobis.
> Agnus dei qui tollis peccata mundi, donna [*sic*] nobis pacem.
> *Das agnus dei sol auch latinisch gesungen werden.*
> *Inclina te.*
> Domine Iesu Christe, qui dixisti apostolis tuis, pacem meam do vobis, pacem meam relinquo
> vobis, ne respicias peccata mea, sed fidem ecclesiae tuae, eamque secundum voluntatem
> tuam pacificare custodire adiuvare et regere digneris, qui cum deo, patre et spiritu
> sancto vivis et regnas deus per omnia saecula saeculorum, amen.
> *Inclinatus dicat.*
> Domine Iesu Christi, fili dei vivi, qui ex voluntate patris cooperante spiritu sancto per
> mortem propriam mundum vivificasti. Libera nos per hoc sacrosanctum corpus et sanguinem
> tuum ab omnibus iniquitatibus et universis malis nostris: Et fac nos tuis semper obedire
> mandatis, et a tua misericordia nunquam in perpetuum separari permittas. Qui vivis et
> regnas deus per omnia saecula saeculorum, amen.
> *ALIA ORATIO.*
> Sacramentum corporis tui domine Iesu Christe, quod nos indigni tui sumere cupimus, non
> nobis proveniat in iudicium et condemnationem, sed pro tua pietate prosit nobis ad
> tutamen mentis et corporis, et ad medelam percipiendam, qui vivis et regnas cum deo patre
> in unitate spiritussancti deus per omnia saecula saeculorum, amen.

O Lamb of God, that takest away the sins of the world, have mercy upon us.
O Lamb of God, that takest away the sins of the world, have mercy upon us.
O Lamb of God, that takest away the sins of the world, grant us peace.
*The Agnus Dei shall also be sung in Latin.*
*Bow thyself.*
O Lord Jesus Christ, who saidst unto thine apostles, My peace I give unto you, my peace I
leave with you: regard not my sins, but the faith of thy Church; and vouchsafe to pacify,
keep, help and govern her according to thy will; who with God the Father and the Holy Ghost
livest and reignest God, world without end. Amen.
*Bowing, let him say:*
O Lord Jesus Christ, Son of the living God, who by the will of the Father, the Holy Ghost
working with thee, hast by thine own death given life to the world: deliver us by this thy
most holy body and blood from all our iniquities and all our evils; and make us ever to obey
thy commandments, and suffer us never to be separated from thy mercy for evermore. Who
livest and reignest God, world without end. Amen.
*Another prayer.*
Let the sacrament of thy body, O Lord Jesus Christ, which we unworthy desire to receive, not
turn to us unto judgement and condemnation; but of thy goodness let it profit us unto the
defence of mind and body, and unto the obtaining of healing; who livest and reignest with
God the Father in the unity of the Holy Ghost, God, world without end. Amen.

**Note.** These are the three prayers the priest said before his communion. Pfalz-Neuburg
1543 (note 20–21) reports that in 1540 two were said, the third being an alternative to the
second.

1. ***Domine Iesu Christe, qui dixisti.***
   - The two halves of John 14:27 are inverted: "do … relinquo", where the Roman text has
     *Pacem relinquo vobis, pacem meam do vobis*.
   - The verbs "pacificare custodire adiuvare et regere" are those of the Canon's *Te
     igitur* (*pacificare, custodire, adunare et regere*), with *adiuvare* for *adunare*.
     The Tridentine text has only *pacificare et coadunare*.
2. ***Fili Dei vivi.***
   - The Roman singular (*libera me … meis … fac me … inhaerere … a te*) is made plural and
     congregational.
   - *Inhaerere* becomes *obedire*.
   - *A te numquam separari* becomes *a tua misericordia nunquam in perpetuum separari*.
   - This petition, "Libera nos per hoc sacrosanctum corpus et sanguinem tuum", is not the
     embolism *Libera nos, quaesumus*.
3. ***Perceptio corporis tui*** appears as *Sacramentum corporis tui*.
   - It is plural, and *praesumo* becomes *cupimus*.
   - *Tutamen* stands for *tutamentum*. The stray *tui* after *indigni* is as printed.

### 12.6 The exhortation (Osiander, Nürnberg 1533)

> *Deinde vertens se ad populum legat hanc adhortationem.*
> Ir allerliebsten in gott, dieweil wir itzt das abendmal unsers herrn Jesu Christi wollen
> bedenken und halten, darin er uns sein fleisch zu einer speis und sein blut zu einem
> trank, den glauben damit zu sterken, gegeben hat, sollen wir billich mit grossem fleiss
> ein jeder sich selbs prüfen, wie der heilig Paulus uns vermanet: denn dis heilig
> sacrament ist zu einem sondern trost und sterk gegeben den armen betrübten gewissen die
> ire sunde bekennen, gottes zorn und den tod forchten und nach der gerechtigkeit hungerig
> und durstig sind. So wir uns aber selbs prüfen und ein jeder in sein eigen gewissen
> gehen, wie uns der heilig Paulus leret, werden wir gewislich nicht anders finden, denn
> allerlei greuliche sund und den tod, den wir mit der sunde verschuldet haben, und können
> doch uns selbs in keinem wege daraus helfen. Darum hat unser lieber herr Jesus Christus
> sich uber uns erbarmet und ist um unser sunde willen mensche worden, auf das er das
> gesetz und allen willen gottes fur uns zu gut erfüllet und den tod und alles, was wir mit
> unsern sunden verschuldet hetten, fur uns und zu unser ledigung auf sich neme und
> erlitte, und das wir je das festiglich glauben und durch den glauben frölich in seinem
> willen mochten leben, nam er nach dem abendmal das brod, sagt dank, brachs und sprach:
> Nemet hin und esset, das ist mein leib, der fur euch dargeben wird, das ist, das ich
> mensch bin worden und alles, das ich thue und leide, ist alles euer eigen, fur euch und
> euch zu gut geschehen; des zu einem gewissen anzeigen und zeugnis gib ich euch mein leib
> zur speise. Desgleichen nam er auch den kelch und sprach: Nemet hin und trinket aus
> diesem alle, das ist der kelch des neuen testaments in meinem blut, das fur euch und fur
> viel vergossen wird zu vergebung der sunde, so oft ir das thut, solt ir mein darbei dabei
> [*sic*] gedenken, das ist, dieweil ich mich eur angenomen und eur sunde auf mich geladen
> hab, will ich mich selbs fur die sunde in tod opfern, mein blut vergiessen, gnad und
> vergebung der sunde erwerben und also ein neu testament aufrichten, darinnen die sunde
> vergeben und ewig nicht mehr sol gedacht werden, des zu einem gewissen anzeigen und
> zeugnis, gib ich euch mein blut zu trinken. Wer nu also von diesem brod isset und von
> diesem kelch trinket, auch diesen worten, so er von Christo höret und diesen zeichen, die
> er von Christo empfahet, festiglich glaubt, der bleibt in dem herrn Christo und Christus
> in im, und wird ewiglich leben; darbei sollen wir nu sein gedenken und seinen tod
> verkündigen, nemlich, das er fur unser sunde sei gestorben und zu unser rechtfertigung
> wider auferstanden und im darum danksagen, ein jeder sein creuz auf sich nemen und im
> nachfolgen und nach seinem gebot einander lieben, wie er uns geliebt hat; denn wir sind
> alle ein brod und ein leib, dieweil wir alle eins brods teilhaftig sind und aus einem
> kelch trinken.

*Then turning himself to the people let him read this exhortation.*
Dearly beloved in God, forasmuch as we will now consider and keep the supper of our Lord
Jesus Christ, wherein he hath given us his flesh for a meat and his blood for a drink,
therewith to strengthen faith, we ought rightly every one with great diligence to examine
himself, as the holy Paul exhorteth us; for this holy sacrament is given for a special
comfort and strength unto the poor troubled consciences that confess their sins, fear God's
wrath and death, and are hungry and thirsty after righteousness. But when we examine
ourselves, and every one goeth into his own conscience, as the holy Paul teacheth us, we
shall surely find nothing else but all manner of horrible sin, and death, which we have
deserved with sin; and yet we cannot in any wise help ourselves out thereof. Therefore hath
our dear Lord Jesus Christ had mercy upon us, and for our sins' sake is become man, to the
end that he might fulfil the law and all the will of God for us and for our good, and take
upon himself and suffer death and all that we had deserved with our sins, for us and for our
deliverance. And that we might steadfastly believe this, and through faith live joyfully in
his will, he took after the supper the bread, gave thanks, brake it, and said: Take and eat,
this is my body, which is given for you; that is, that I am become man, and all that I do
and suffer is all your own, done for you and for your good; for a sure token and witness
whereof I give you my body for meat. Likewise he took also the cup, and said: Take and drink
ye all of this, this is the cup of the new testament in my blood, which is shed for you and
for many for the remission of sins; as oft as ye do this, ye shall remember me thereby; that
is, forasmuch as I have taken your cause upon me and laden your sins upon myself, I will
offer myself unto death for sin, shed my blood, obtain grace and forgiveness of sins, and so
set up a new testament, wherein sins are forgiven and shall be remembered no more for ever;
for a sure token and witness whereof I give you my blood to drink. Whosoever now eateth thus
of this bread and drinketh of this cup, and firmly believeth these words which he heareth of
Christ and these signs which he receiveth of Christ, he dwelleth in the Lord Christ and
Christ in him, and shall live for ever. Hereby shall we now remember him and show forth his
death, namely that he died for our sins and rose again for our justification, and give him
thanks therefor; every one take up his cross and follow him, and according to his
commandment love one another, as he hath loved us: for we are all one bread and one body,
seeing we are all partakers of one bread and drink of one cup.

**Note.** This is Osiander's Nürnberg exhortation (§8.7) as revised for Brandenburg-Nürnberg
1533 (Sehling 11, 195). It comes after the Latin priest's prayers and immediately before the
distribution. *Darbei dabei* is a doublet in the print.

### 12.7 Communion

> *Darauf sol angefangen werden das responsorium discubuit Jesus latinisch und ob der
> communicanten viel weren, das man damit nicht zureichen mocht, sol man dem volk deudsch
> anfahen zu singen, gott sei gelobet, oder Jesus Christus, unser heiland, welcher gesenge
> einer, ob auch gleich das discubuit zureichet, dennoch gleichwol nach der communion sol
> gesungen werden.*
> *Nunc communicantes accedunt et cum eis corpus dominicum porrigitur dicat sacerdos.*
> Nim hin und iss, das ist der leib Christi, der fur dich geben ist.
> *Ad calicem dicat diaconus.*
> Nim hin und trink, das ist das blut des neuen testaments, das fur deine sunde vergossen
> ist.

*Thereupon shall be begun the responsory Discubuit Jesus in Latin; and if the communicants
were many, so that it might not suffice therewith, one shall begin to sing to the people in
German "Gott sei gelobet" or "Jesus Christus, unser Heiland"; one of which songs, even though
the Discubuit suffice, shall nevertheless be sung after the communion.*
*Now the communicants approach, and when the Lord's body is reached to them, let the priest
say:*
Take and eat, this is the body of Christ, which is given for thee.
*At the cup let the deacon say:*
Take and drink, this is the blood of the new testament, which is shed for thy sins.

**Note.** The communion formula is Lutheran, not Roman. *Corpus Domini nostri … custodiat*
is not used. The Latin responsory *Discubuit Jesus* (from the Maundy Thursday office) serves
as the communion chant.

### 12.8 Thanksgiving and the two Latin ablution prayers

> *Deinde vertens se ad populum legat sequentem graciarum actionem.*
> Last uns bitten.
> O almechtiger ewiger gott, wir sagen deiner göttlichen mildigkeit lob und dank, das du uns
> mit deinem heilsamen fleisch und blut deines einigen sons Jesu Christi, unsers herrn,
> gespeiset und getrenket hast, und bitten dich demütiglich, du wollest durch deinen
> heiligen geist in uns wirken, wie wir dis heilig sacrament mit dem munde haben empfangen,
> das wir auch also dein göttlich gnad, vergebung der sunde, vereinigung mit Christo und
> ewigs leben, so darinnen angezeigt und zugesagt ist, mit festem glauben mögen begreifen
> und ewiglich behalten. Wir danken dir auch, allmechtiger gott, das du uns durch diese
> heilsame gabe des leibes und bluts Christi, deines sons, hast erquicket, und bitten dein
> barmherzigkeit, das du uns solchs gedeihen lassest zum starken glauben gegen dir und zu
> brünstiger lieb unter uns allen, durch unsern herrn Jesum Christum, deinen son, der mit
> dir in ewigkeit des heiligen geists lebt und herschet, warer gott imer und ewiglich,
> amen.
> *Deinde inclinet se et dicat.*
> Corpus tuum domine quod nos peccatores sumpsimus, et calix quem potauimus, adhaereat
> visceribus nostris, et praesta omnipotens deus, ut ibi nulla remaneat peccati macula, ubi
> tua pura et sancta introierunt sacramenta, per eundem Christum dominum nostrum.
> Quod ore sumpsimus domine pura mente capiamus, et fiat nobis remedium sempiternum, per
> Christum dominum nostrum.
